- 2026-08-04 #9849 [css-pseudo-4]
  RESOLVED: Clarify that spacing is about layout-introduced spacing, not about spacing characters (which are just considered text). Update WPTs
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/9849#issuecomment-5179968636
  background: I believe the spec should be explicit about which, if any, ASCII control characters should be potential targets of highlight pseudos. For reference, the CSS Text Decorations spec says "Characters belonging to the Unicode classes for control codes and unassigned characters (Cc, Cf, Cn) are skipped...". That includes tabs.

- 2026-08-04 #12594 [css-navigation-1,css-view-transitions-2]
  RESOLVED: Drop 'with' from the spec for now, open an issue with motivating examples to add it back in when we have those
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/12594#issuecomment-5178098027
  background: Following from a past discussion around navigation-matching for view-transitions in #8925, see [explainer](https://github.com/WICG/declarative-partial-updates/blob/main/route-matching-explainer.md#css-navigations-1) and the [css-navigation-1](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-navigation-1/) emerging spec.
  related: #8925 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/8925
  irc (60 lines):
    | <noamr> https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/12594#issuecomment-5118003200
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: Issue covers a lot but I've narrowed it down in one of the comments, it's all connected
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: We discussed this first a year ago in the summer f2f
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: This is about being able to select links and create conditionals based on the nav state - you're currently navigating to/from a given URL, and other info about the nav
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: treating the nav state as any other DOM state, like :hover or @media
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: There are some things in the ED that are more finished than others
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: I want to focus on URLs, URL patterns, and routes
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: there are links in the comment to those parts of the spec
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: So we want to add a condition based on which URL you're going to or from, and condition a link with a pseudo-class based on whether it's pointing to a URL we're going to/from
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: how this works is, the main useful thing is the @navigation rule
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: This is a conditional that only applies while there is an active nav
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: the contents of the rule let you narrow down the nav based on aspects of it
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: the main use is setting up View Transitions in a particular way
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: Bramus can maybe show off a demo
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: So @nav is a conditional, you're going to or from a URL, the current URL is /something/, etc
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: the URLPattern function lets you provide a url-pattern inline in a selector or conditional
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: the 'route' rule lets you name a pattern so it can be reused
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: The :link-to() pseudo class lets you select a link based on its routing
    | <bramus> Demo (+ a write-up of the core concepts): https://www.bram.us/2026/07/30/styling-the-navigation-declarative-route-and-navigation-matching-in-css/
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: This is about it as an intro. Hope it's pretty straightforward, hoping to hear opinions
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: There are other things in the spec about phases and history, dont' have to touch on them right now
    | <JakeA> q+
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: open qs are base URL of URL patterns, seems like most people expect them to be from the document, but that can't happen for no-cors cross-origin stylesheets, would be a bit exotic
    | <astearns> ack JakeA
    | <TabAtkins> JakeA: can you talk more about the no-cors issue and how it differs from other URL usages?
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: all URLs in stylesheets are relative to the *stylesheet's* URL, not the document
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: with route this is a bit weird, probably. maybe it's okay, you can define your route in an inline stylesheet and use it later. but in general you want to be relative to the document where the stylesheet was loaded from
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: but if you're an external stylesheet without CORS, you don't even know what origin included you
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: this would expose info to 3p stylesheets that currently aren't exposed
    | <astearns> q+
    | <emilio> q+
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: but to know - you're not just exposing the doc URL, you're having to match against it purposely
    | <TabAtkins> JakeA: People will expect it to be doc-relative, i agree. Would be real gotcha if they develop on a local dev server then deploy to a CDN and things break because it's suddenly a 3p origin
    | <bramus> +1
    | <TabAtkins> JakeA: The no-cors thing will be a gotcha, but if it's just a fact of security, then ok
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: I'd like to ask a dumb question
    | <JakeA> q+
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: you say these @nav rules are mainly for VTs
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: before @nav is available, they'll likely just be directly in the stylesheet
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: Is there thought about how those interact?
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: yes. one of the reasons we wanted to pursue this is you can't do this routing inside of a view-transition rule
    | <astearns> ack astearns
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: today people do one VT rule and then do all the work inside the JS event, page reveal events
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: it's finicky to do that
    | <bramus> https://gist.github.com/bramus/33fbbbbf0e94f1a46a43df818e9d8ee5 has a code example
    | <bramus> (to be included in the spec later)
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: The way they'd interact now is you put the view-transition rules inside of the @nav
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: In the transition period where @nav is partially supported...
    | <emilio> ack emilio
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: you probably want to use a supports query to know when you should do it all yourself
    | <astearns> ack JakeA
    | <TabAtkins> JakeA: with the nav stuff, can you explain different between at/with, and from/to
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: You're navigating *from* a place *to* a place
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: at/with is the current URL and the other url
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: If you want to set up a VT that's symmetrical, if I'm on the homepage I want this particular element to have this VT name, and I don't care which direction I'm going to or from homepage...
    | <TabAtkins> noamr: So whenever you're navigating you have a direction relationship, from/to, and a current/other relationship
    | <bramus> https://gist.github.com/bramus/131579284afb527c7b50d1fe7c240676 shows an example
    | <TabAtkins> (i'm confused by that description)
    | <TabAtkins> bramus: I have an example here
    | <TabAtkins> bramus: When you're navigating from an overview to a detail, some CSS applies just at the overview page, some at the details page

- 2026-08-04 #13608 [animation-triggers-1]
  RESOLVED: Adopt timeline-trigger-range as shorthand
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13608#issuecomment-5180367797
  background: When specifying a range for a timeline-trigger, there are the `timeline-trigger-activation-range` and `timeline-trigger-active-range` properties.
  irc (46 lines):
    | <fantasai> bramus: For timeline triggers, we have a shorthand which accepts trigger name, source, activation range, and active range
    | <fantasai> bramus: We also have longhands for each of those
    | <fantasai> bramus: But two properties set ranges
    | <fantasai> bramus: One request from authors, which I also caught myself, was to have a property to set both ranges in one declaration
    | <fantasai> bramus: This would mirror what's allowed in the shorthands
    | <fantasai> bramus: This gets complicated with the naming
    | <fantasai> bramus: timeline-trigger-ranges would not really fit with how we expand without pluralization
    | <fantasai> bramus: maybe it's ok?
    | <bramus> fantasai: You could jus tleave it singular
    | <flackr> +1
    | <bramus> … in a number of cases authors could set them to the same thing
    | <bramus> scribe+
    | <fantasai> s/could/would/
    | <fantasai> bramus: not sure set that often to the same thing
    | <fantasai> bramus: e.g. scroll-triggered animations
    | <fantasai> flackr: I think it's enough cases the same that it'll be common
    | <fantasai> flackr: whenever using alternate/reverse mode you typically want the ranges to be the same
    | <fantasai> ydaniv: Also, I think we already have slash in the syntax for the active range
    | <fantasai> ydaniv: So not so much a different range, but an extension
    | <fantasai> ydaniv: So it's the same range, but extended
    | <fantasai> SebastianZ: activation vs active?
    | <fantasai> ydaniv: activation range you have to enter to trigger the animation
    | <fantasai> ydaniv: and active range is the range that it's active in, once you exit it ends
    | <fantasai> fantasai: active range is often larger than the activation range
    | <fantasai> fantasai: e.g. activate in middle third of screen, active as long as visible
    | <bramus> Demo: https://codepen.io/bramus/pen/PwNLzqE
    | <fantasai> DavidA: Part of motivation for 'active' name is the active pseudo-class, when element gains :active it gets larger and then stays active range until focus out
    | <fantasai> SebastianZ: we basically always have singular, right?
    | <fantasai> SebastianZ: so I would keep singular
    | <fantasai> SebastianZ: Only concern I have is that as an author, you probably will be confused which will be which
    | <fantasai> bramus: Already have that in shorthand. Using a slash.
    | <bramus> fantasai: you activate first, so that comes first
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Adopt timeline-trigger-range as shorthand
    | <fantasai> bramus: personally lean towards plural
    | <fantasai> SebastianZ: we only have one case which is 'columns'
    | <fantasai> ydaniv: the initial is 'auto'
    | <flackr> I lean towards singular
    | <fantasai> POLL: singular or pluarl?
    | <flackr> we don't have margins or paddings :-)
    | <fantasai> s/pluarl/plural/
    | <bramus> pluarl but spelled correctly :)
    | <SebastianZ> singular
    | <fantasai> singular
    | <flackr> singular
    | <ydaniv> don't mind
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Adopt timeline-trigger-range as shorthand
- 2026-08-04 #14060 [animation-triggers-1]
  RESOLVED: Reset does not fire AnimationStart or AnimationEnd events
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14060#issuecomment-5180537907
  background: The AnimationTrigger [`reset`](https://drafts.csswg.org/animation-triggers-1/#valdef-animation-action-reset) action pauses the animation and sets its currentTime to 0. This will often have the effect of transitioning the animation from its [after phase](https://www.w3.org/TR/web-animations-1/#animation-effect-after-phase) (when it has finished) to its [before](https://www.w3.org/TR/web-animations-
  irc (58 lines):
    | <fantasai> DavidA: The 'reset' sets an animation back to its initial state
    | <fantasai> DavidA: e.g. scroll something out of view, it resets to start, and when scroll back into view it starts up again
    | <fantasai> DavidA: Currently this shifts currentTime to zero
    | <fantasai> DavidA: but this changes the phase of the animation from the "after" phase to the "before" phas
    | <fantasai> DavidA: Usually when transitioning between theses phases, will dispatch events
    | <fantasai> DavidA: Per spec, this will fire AnimationStart and AnimationEnd events
    | <flackr> q+
    | <fantasai> DavidA: Suggest to suppress the events for this action.
    | <ydaniv> +1
    | <fantasai> ydaniv: You're not starting the animations, just resetting
    | <fantasai> DavidA: Might want *some* event to associate with this
    | <fantasai> DavidA: but seems different than AnimationStart and AnimationEnd
    | <bramus> fantasai: maybe you want an animation reset or restart
    | <fantasai> SebastianZ: what happens if you call reset in the middle of an animation?
    | <bramus> ydaniv: trigger could happen mid animation
    | <fantasai> ydaniv: if the action resets then it resets
    | <JohnJansen> q?
    | <fantasai> DavidA: It would reset from active phase to before phase
    | <fantasai> DavidA: That action is supposed to trigger an AnimationEnd event
    | <fantasai> flackr: Reason why Animations spec has these triggers is that, assumption is, if you had a time-based animation and it went from after to before, you must have played the animation
    | <JohnJansen> ack flackr
    | <fantasai> flackr: but that's not true for this reset case, so I see reason to make an exception here
    | <fantasai> flackr: I think should be consistent with what happens if you were to seek the animation with JS to the position before playing
    | <fantasai> flackr: because that's conceptually what you're doing
    | <fantasai> fantasai: that makes sense to me
    | <fantasai> fantasai: Does that mean if you reset mid-animation, it doesn't fire AnimationEn
    | <fantasai> flackr: exactly
    | <fantasai> SebastianZ: Maybe there should be an event fired like AnimationReset
    | <fantasai> SebastianZ: you know when you're in the middle of the animation, you know that the reset happened
    | <fantasai> flackr: We could define an event. But different issue
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Reset does not fire AnimationStart or AnimationEnd events
    | <fantasai> flackr: Is that consistent with case where animation has finished and you call reset?
    | <fantasai> fantasai: If it's not going to call these, then it should never call it.
    | <fantasai> flackr: You're setting up to run again.
    | <fantasai> flackr: Shouldn't it fire AnimationStart?
    | <fantasai> flackr: It's an active animation, so similar to animation with animation-delay
    | <fantasai> ydaniv: Like adding epsilon delay
    | <bramus> fantasai: so if I have a animation that is not running and is not in the running state … does reset kick off running?
    | <fantasai> SebastianZ: when in the middle of running the animation, call reset, do we run again?
    | <fantasai> flackr: It doesn't progress, just moves back to start
    | <fantasai> SebastianZ: Then I would say no event
    | <fantasai> flackr: If you create a CSS animation that's play-state = paused, I believe it fires ANimationStart because you've created an animation and it has an effect (the before state)
    | <fantasai> DavidA: But I think it's different because that animation is in the active phase
    | <fantasai> DavidA: But here we're in the before phase
    | <fantasai> flackr: If you create an animation with an animation-delay, it'll fire before the delay
    | <fantasai> ydaniv: If you set fill-backwards or not?
    | <fantasai> flackr: that shouldn't affect events
    | <fantasai> ydaniv: I think this is why we chose to put it in the before phase
    | <fantasai> flackr: Right, so that dev can control whether it has an effect or not
    | <fantasai> DavidA: Wouldn't the AnimationStart be when you cross the active boundary?
    | <fantasai> DavidA: That's why paused fires it. Even though you paused, it's still in the active phase
    | <fantasai> flackr: Nevermind, AnimationStart fires after the delay, so I'm wrong!
    | <fantasai> SebastianZ: Discussing reset?
    | <fantasai> SebastianZ: but replay is reset + play again. Then the event should be triggered, right?
    | <fantasai> Agreed.
    | <fantasai> flackr: You get the event when you start playing, but until then no event
    | <fantasai> flackr: Sorry!
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Reset does not fire AnimationStart or AnimationEnd events
- 2026-08-04 #14129 [animation-triggers-1]
  RESOLVED: Align supported events with the events we have in DOM
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14129#issuecomment-5181442750
  background: Currently the spec for values allowed as [`<event-trigger-event>`](https://drafts.csswg.org/animation-triggers-1/#typedef-event-trigger-event) is practically placeholder:
  irc (60 lines):
    | <bramus> ydaniv: tab put this in the spec, but as a sort of placeholder. what are the actual events we want to spec for event trigger?
    | <bramus> … there was activate in there (which I think is great) as its sort of like wha tth eplatform has for an accesible click
    | <bramus> … would like to add interest to that (also well defined on the platform)
    | <flackr> q+
    | <bramus> … I also use those names in my code when I implement these
    | <bramus> … and also — I have proposals — start with limited set and expand later
    | <bramus> … keyboard/mouse/pointer/interest/activate
    | <JohnJansen> ack flackr
    | <bramus> flackr: what is the differnce between activate and click?
    | <bramus> ydaniv: activate is the accesible click
    | <bramus> flackr: but that fires a click, no?
    | <DavidA> q+
    | <bramus> ydaniv: if you have a keyboard press (enter or space) it also fires a click?
    | <bramus> flackr: on specific elements
    | <bramus> … dont understand the specific need for it, as it’s the same as click
    | <bramus> ydaniv: I dont want ppl to have to … I think tab and elika already put it in there.
    | <bramus> … also disambiguous for users
    | <DavidA> q-
    | <bramus> … can work for all elements
    | <bramus> flackr: what I want to do, is have this correspond to JS events you can listen to
    | <bramus> … so mildly concerned about the concept of activation
    | <bramus> … e.g. how would you listen for that outside of an animation trigger?
    | <bramus> ydaniv: I guess with both event listeners
    | <bramus> … both click and keyboard press
    | <bramus> … and the latter also requires tabindex, right?
    | <bramus> flackr: I don’t think …
    | <SebastianZ> q+
    | <bramus> ydaniv: if you have an element that can trigger an activatoin, it has to be an element like a button or input
    | <bramus> … but if you want a div, then you need tabindex
    | <bramus> bramus: that}s for keyboard focusable scrollers
    | <bramus> flackr: we should not define activation, and lean on existing concepts
    | <bramus> +1

- 2026-08-04 #14181 [scroll-animations-1] -> [[scroll-driven-animations]]
  RESOLVED: Start specifying animation-range: stuck
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14181#issuecomment-5176689299
  background: Using scroll-driven animations in conjunction with a `sticky` positioned layout is quite common, especially in "scrolltelling". It's often desired to tie the start of a range to the scroll position when an element becomes "stuck", and tie the end of the range to the position when it becomes "unstuck".
  irc (51 lines):
    | <kbabbitt> ydaniv: this is a simple proposal
    | <kbabbitt> ... already have stuck for scroll state
    | <kbabbitt> ... notion of stuck is already well defined
    | <kbabbitt> ... stuck wehn talking about scroll animations, it's something that we've given a lot of use cases
    | <kbabbitt> ... anything that has to do with scrollytelling
    | <TabAtkins> scrolltelling????
    | <lea> scrollytelling
    | <kbabbitt> ... example, most common one, stuff moving horizontally while moving vertically
    | <kbabbitt> ... want to move stuff only once container is stuck
    | <kbabbitt> ... like in stickypos
    | <kbabbitt> ... if you don't have a way to just say I want my timeline / active range to be just when subject is stuck
    | <kbabbitt> ... then you have to make sure you have insets, or other fixed pos stuff, to take out of the viewport, have to manage around it
    | <kbabbitt> ... many times outside that element
    | <kizu> q+
    | <kbabbitt> ... makes common cases which repeats a lot for scrolling animations, makes it very simple
    | <kbabbitt> ... only thing that might make this more tricky is when you have sticky of start and end
    | <kbabbitt> ... need to see whether we need special definition for that
    | <kbabbitt> ... whether it makes case more complex
    | <bramus> q+
    | <astearns> ack kizu
    | <kbabbitt> kizu: +1 to this, one other use case is similar to sutck queries
    | <kbabbitt> ... when you want to change from non-stuck to stuck version
    | <kbabbitt> ... this would allow doing this relatively easy
    | <kbabbitt> ... can do with scroll driven animations and view timelines but need to know exaclty where element will get stuck in viewport
    | <kbabbitt> ... if you know exact range where it's stuck, wold be nice to use that info for this use case
    | <kbabbitt> ... scroll driven animation use cases, I was working around stuck use case by manually adjusting view timeline
    | <astearns> ack bramus
    | <kbabbitt> bramus: +1 to the use case
    | <kbabbitt> ... one thing I was wondering was, should this be a range name or type of timeline where you have "stuck timeline" so to speak
    | <kbabbitt> ... upside clear to reason about it, downside, would be more tricky to achieve animations from moment element is stuck until it has exited scrollport
    | <kbabbitt> ... start range would target stuck stage, end range would target contain stage
    | <kbabbitt> ... separate stuck range would need to do internal translation, stuck 0% change to contain <top/bottom pos value>
    | <kbabbitt> ... I can write down code
    | <astearns> ack fantasai
    | <kbabbitt> fantasai: I think this makes a lot of sense
    | <kbabbitt> ... is not a separate timeline I think
    | <kbabbitt> ... short way of describing a view timeline range start or end pos based on other properties you set
    | <bramus> Example: Element stuck at `bottom 20px`:  with a `stuck` range: `stuck 0% contain 100%`. Without a stuck range: `contain 20px contain 100%`.
    | <kbabbitt> astearns: should we resolve on animation-range stuck?
    | <kbabbitt> ... or bramus would you rather take some time?
    | <kbabbitt> bramus: fine with either
    | <kbabbitt> astearns: other comments or questions?
    | <kbabbitt> fantasai: one question, should this apply when container doesn't have scroll snap set? I think yes
    | <kbabbitt> ... not really stuck but you set a range of properties where it should be stuck
    | <kbabbitt> ... should still apply I think
    | <kbabbitt> bramus: how is this related to snapping?
    | <kbabbitt> ... isn't this about pos: sticky top bottom value?
    | <kbabbitt> fantasai: never mind
    | <kbabbitt> astearns: Proposed: Start specifying animation-range: stuck
    | <fantasai> I think I mixed up two different things
    | <kbabbitt> RESOLVED: Start specifying animation-range: stuck
- 2026-08-04 #14212 [css-mixins-1]
  RESOLVED: in function bodies, function and mix in arguments, and all private blocks !important is invalid and causes declaration to be invalid
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14212#issuecomment-5180899628
  background: https://drafts.csswg.org/css-mixins-1/#function-rule specifies that: - the default value of a parameter is `<declaration-value>` which does not accept `!important` - the function body is `<declaration-rule-list>` which *could* accept `!important` on declarations
  irc (14 lines):
    | <sgill> kbabbitt: do we accept !important in body of function and mix in
    | <sgill> ... either in parameters or results
    | <sgill> ... results makes sense
    | <lea> q+
    | <sgill> ... parameters and locals doesn't do anything but for author ergonomics it is not great if it doesn't work
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: lean weakly towards making them invalid where they are not cascading
    | <lea> q-
    | <lea> weak preference towards just making them work, even if a little silly
    | <sgill> lea: case for making it invalid if we plan to making it mean something later
    | <sgill> astearns: weakly in favor of following precedent
    | <tuankiet65> +1 on that
    | <sgill> PROPOSED: in function bodies, function and mix in arguments, and all privates !important is invalid and causes declaration to be invalid
    | <kbabbitt> s/all privates/all @private blocks/
    | <astearns> RESOLVED: in function bodies, function and mix in arguments, and all private blocks !important is invalid and causes declaration to be invalid
- 2026-08-04 #14224 [-]
  RESOLVED: Unless there is good use case based motivation, the minimum to support is hyphen-asterisk for prefixes. We can explore potential relaxing on a case by case basis based on use cases, but it would need to be well motivated.
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14224#issuecomment-5177254686
  background: This is meant as a parent issue to track selective wildcarding of parts of css-syntax: classes, attributes, CSS properties, whatever. Tracking these issues together is important to end up with an architecturally coherent design, rather than individual ad hoc solutions.
  irc (60 lines):
    | <kbabbitt> lea: there are various cases around wildcarding in CSS that keep coming up
    | <kbabbitt> ... class prefixes, element prefixes for specific custom elements
    | <kbabbitt> ... attribute prefixes for libs that add them
    | <kbabbitt> .... wildcards in custom props
    | <kbabbitt> ... this is not about whether we should do any particular one, how we store prefixed selectors etc.
    | <kbabbitt> ... those will be discussed in individual issues
    | <oriol> q+
    | <kbabbitt> ... but if we decide on these ad hoc we're likely to end up with multiple different syntax and no overall coherence
    | <kbabbitt> ... I think it makes sense to resolve towards syntaxes as a design principle
    | <kbabbitt> ... CSS already has wildcarding if you consider * selector a wildcard
    | <kbabbitt> ... any wildcarding we introduce is a generalization of that
    | <kbabbitt> ... if you have .foo-* it might look like other things if there isn't whitespace
    | <kbabbitt> ... we already have selectors that behave differently based on whitespace so I think that's ok
    | <TabAtkins> q+
    | <kbabbitt> .... I think we should resolve to use * for wildcards unless there's a reason not to
    | <kbabbitt> ... instead of introducing new pseudos for every wildcard we might want to introduce
    | <kbabbitt> ... a lot of use cases are around matching prefixes of hyphen separated things
    | <kbabbitt> ... all the current issues have this in common
    | <kbabbitt> ... in custom props, selectors, classes, attributes
    | <kbabbitt> ... use cases in wild are hyphen separated
    | <SebastianZ> q+
    | <kbabbitt> ... want to match prefix of parts that are hyphen separated usually in the beginning
    | <florian> q+
    | <kbabbitt> ... if we can scope to that, allows optimizations in engines, makes it more likely to be implemented
    | <kbabbitt> ... this issue is about, are there any objectoins to scoping down to this?
    | <romain> q+
    | <kbabbitt> ... having hyphen has user facing benefits as well
    | <kbabbitt> ... makes it less likely for authors to match substrings they didn't intend
    | <kbabbitt> ... I think when there is such a concentration of use cases around hyphen separated prefixes might make sense to scope MVP around that
    | <lea> q?
    | <kbabbitt> ... this issue is just about syntax, not saying we're going to do any particular type of prefix, just to pave the way for those so we don't have to decide on syntax for each individual case
    | <astearns> ack oriol
    | <kbabbitt> oriol: for most if not all of sub issues there are different approaches that seem better to me than just using *
    | <kbabbitt> ... would prefer to go case by case instead of now resolving to use this syntax
    | <kbabbitt> ... which seems worse and more limited
    | <kbabbitt> ... for example matching tag names
    | <lea> q+
    | <kbabbitt> ... if we use a functional pseudo instead of *, could add multiple extensions for case sensitivity etc
    | <kbabbitt> ... currently depends on language
    | <kbabbitt> ... if we add some extension to not just match identically but allow subsets, we could extend and allow optimizations in functions for that
    | <kbabbitt> ... also we already have a syntax for matching substrings that we use in attribute values
    | <kbabbitt> ... would prefer for consistency to reuse this existing syntax
    | <kbabbitt> ... already said multiple times in issue, mentioned adding a space in a selector
    | <kbabbitt> ... that's not the concern I have whe I say the * looks like universal selector
    | <kbabbitt> ... my concern is not about spaces. my concern is that in compound selector, simple selectors can be reordered freely
    | <kbabbitt> ... except for [missed]
    | <florian> q?
    | <kbabbitt> ... even if technically we don't have this problem now it could bring confusion
    | <kbabbitt> ... asterisk at end could be universal selector that was reordered instead of allowing class of something
    | <kbabbitt> ... have some concerns in general, my preference would be to go case by case, not convinced this is best approach
    | <astearns> ack TabAtkins
    | <kbabbitt> TabAtkins: disagree fairly strongly with what oriol said
    | <kbabbitt> ... even going case by case I think * at end is the best syntax for all of these cases
    | <lea> +1 TabAtkins
    | <kbabbitt> ... common usage already even in casual usage
    | <bramus> +1
    | <kbabbitt> ... people aren't confused by * at end, it means wildcard, in contexts outside CSS
    | <kbabbitt> ... especially if we limit to dashed forms, foo-* is super clear about what it means
    | <ChrisL> +1
    | <tuankiet65> q+
- 2026-08-04 #14243 [css-mixins-1,css-mixins-2]
  RESOLVED: Drop @result, use @private for mixin locals and the rest of the mixin body is the result
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14243#issuecomment-5180782117
  background: This is a parent issue to discuss five interlinked `css-mixins-1` threads together at the F2F. They are really *one* design conversation about **how a mixin is authored and how its arguments/locals resolve**. Tracking them jointly matters so we land an architecturally coherent design rather than five ad hoc decisions.
  related: #13454 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13454, #13524 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13524, #13680 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13680, #13727 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13727, #14004 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14004
  irc (60 lines):
    | <sgill> scribe+
    | <sgill> kbabbitt: set of ideas that decouple two aspects of mixins
    | <sgill> ... 1.) mixing in and 2.) local variables and mix ins
    | <sgill> ... if we have an idea to do hygienic locals and we adopt that as way to specify locals to mix ins
    | <sgill> ... we would flip the way mix ins would get specified
    | <sgill> ... right now everything is local unless in @result, we would be flipping that
    | <sgill> ... solves several problems. if you are not using parameters you do not need @results rule. don't need @macro anymore. gives us a tool to do lexically scoped variables in css rules in general
    | <TabAtkins> instead of `@mixin --foo { --local: x; @result { color: var(--x); }}`, we have `@mixin --local { @locals { --local: x; } color: var(--x); }`
    | <TabAtkins> argh, `@mixin --foo` at the end there
    | <TabAtkins> dang text autocomplete is messing with me
    | <romain> q+
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: the way mix ins handle arguments and locals is we create custom properties on the applying element but their names are rewritten to unobservable non clashing names
    | <sgill> .... any references to variable names in mix in are rewritten
    | <sgill> ... let's us reuse custom property machinery
    | <emilio> q+
    | <sgill> ... prevents you from clashing on other things on the page
    | <kbabbitt> q+
    | <sgill> ... locals would do the same thing essentially
    | <sgill> ... if you use @locals nested inside style rule, will only contain custom properties, and they would have their names rewritten
    | <JakeA> q+
    | <sgill> ... any references to them in the style rules would also be rewritten
    | <lea> +1 to all of this, obvs (as someone who proposed some of it). Bikeshedding: I think `@private` is much clearer than `@locals`
    | <sgill> ... let's you broadly use custom properties for any local temporary variables
    | <sgill> ... mix ins would just be using the @locals rule in the same way it works anywhere else. mix in body is a style rule and written in the standard way
    | <sgill> ... name should not be exposed in any way to the page
    | <astearns> q+
    | <JakeA> q-
    | <astearns> ack romain
    | <sgill> romain: one thing I don't like about locals is that they are not really local to that rule
    | <sgill> ... local to some named implicit parent
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: they are local to the enclosing rule
    | <sgill> romain: don't understand how it would work outside mix ins
    | <sgill> ... one pain point is everyone is defining properties, keyframes and want to namespace them so there are no naming conflicts
    | <sgill> ... would imagine something like that would be named @local or @private
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: syntax here is specialized to custom properties
    | <kizu> Hmm, what if `@locals { @keyframes foo {} }`?
    | <sgill> romain: might be worth still taking into consideration
    | <sgill> ... if it inside the style rule you cannot put key frames inside the style rule?
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: would be able to put key frames inside a locals rule
    | <kizu> q+
    | <astearns> ack emilio
    | <sgill> Emilio: rewriting part feels sketchy to me
    | <sgill> ... if we need to treat them as regular custom properties we should define how we generate that
    | <sgill> ... maybe we track them out of band or something?
    | <astearns> ack kbabbitt
    | <JakeA> q+
    | <sgill> scribe+
    | <sgill> kbabbitt: wanted to talk about implementation. we had someone prototype and we do what @emlio suggested
    | <sgill> ... not opposed to rewrite them in standardized way
    | <sgill> Emilio: was wondering if we were rewriting as custom properties and had a side effect of being exposed, maybe we don't do the rewriting and specify it as an internal local function that takes a name? during resolution we bring in local
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: issue is naming is lexical at the stylesheet level
    | <sgill> Emilio: you would still define them as custom properties but still need some other concept
    | <sgill> kbabbitt: if I have two different rules that define @local baz those are two different local variables and need to both be rewritten but not connected to each other
    | <sgill> Emilio: if you have two ancestors with a foo with some locals would you pick the closest one to inherit?
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: *some drawing on the board*
    | <keithamus> whiteboard:
    | <keithamus>   .foo { @locals { --x: blue } &.bar { color: var(--x) } & + three { color: var(--x) }
    | <kbabbitt> In the example above, the var(--x) reference in `three` gets rewritten to match the defined local --x, but it can't see the value because the value doesn't inherit to a sibling
    | <JakeA> I need to step out, but could we consider inverting the wrapping? As in `@locals { --x: blue; .foo {} }` - this means you can share properties without them being part of the same parent, and it also fixes the 'locals' naming.
    | <astearns> q?

    | <sgill> ... if it inside the style rule you cannot put key frames inside the style rule?
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: would be able to put key frames inside a locals rule
    | <kizu> q+
    | <astearns> ack emilio
    | <sgill> Emilio: rewriting part feels sketchy to me
    | <sgill> ... if we need to treat them as regular custom properties we should define how we generate that
    | <sgill> ... maybe we track them out of band or something?
    | <astearns> ack kbabbitt
    | <JakeA> q+
    | <sgill> scribe+
    | <sgill> kbabbitt: wanted to talk about implementation. we had someone prototype and we do what @emlio suggested
    | <sgill> ... not opposed to rewrite them in standardized way
    | <sgill> Emilio: was wondering if we were rewriting as custom properties and had a side effect of being exposed, maybe we don't do the rewriting and specify it as an internal local function that takes a name? during resolution we bring in local
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: issue is naming is lexical at the stylesheet level
    | <sgill> Emilio: you would still define them as custom properties but still need some other concept
    | <sgill> kbabbitt: if I have two different rules that define @local baz those are two different local variables and need to both be rewritten but not connected to each other
    | <sgill> Emilio: if you have two ancestors with a foo with some locals would you pick the closest one to inherit?
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: *some drawing on the board*
    | <keithamus> whiteboard:
    | <keithamus>   .foo { @locals { --x: blue } &.bar { color: var(--x) } & + three { color: var(--x) }
    | <kbabbitt> In the example above, the var(--x) reference in `three` gets rewritten to match the defined local --x, but it can't see the value because the value doesn't inherit to a sibling
    | <JakeA> I need to step out, but could we consider inverting the wrapping? As in `@locals { --x: blue; .foo {} }` - this means you can share properties without them being part of the same parent, and it also fixes the 'locals' naming.
    | <astearns> q?
- 2026-08-04 #14243 [css-mixins-1,css-mixins-2]
  RESOLVED: specify @private that gets nested inside style rules for now
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14243#issuecomment-5180782117
  background: This is a parent issue to discuss five interlinked `css-mixins-1` threads together at the F2F. They are really *one* design conversation about **how a mixin is authored and how its arguments/locals resolve**. Tracking them jointly matters so we land an architecturally coherent design rather than five ad hoc decisions.
  related: #13454 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13454, #13524 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13524, #13680 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13680, #13727 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13727, #14004 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14004
  irc (60 lines):
    | <sgill> scribe+
    | <sgill> kbabbitt: set of ideas that decouple two aspects of mixins
    | <sgill> ... 1.) mixing in and 2.) local variables and mix ins
    | <sgill> ... if we have an idea to do hygienic locals and we adopt that as way to specify locals to mix ins
    | <sgill> ... we would flip the way mix ins would get specified
    | <sgill> ... right now everything is local unless in @result, we would be flipping that
    | <sgill> ... solves several problems. if you are not using parameters you do not need @results rule. don't need @macro anymore. gives us a tool to do lexically scoped variables in css rules in general
    | <TabAtkins> instead of `@mixin --foo { --local: x; @result { color: var(--x); }}`, we have `@mixin --local { @locals { --local: x; } color: var(--x); }`

    | <sgill> .... any references to variable names in mix in are rewritten

    | <sgill> ... name should not be exposed in any way to the page

- 2026-08-05 #5675 [css-grid-3] -> [[masonry]]
  RESOLVED: Draft this proposal as an experimental, informative appendix of Grid L3 and collect feedback.
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/5675#issuecomment-5195085874
  background: Assuming all authors code their masonry grid in a valid reading order, screen reader user who are non-visual will have no problem with it. But once we introduce visual screen reader users, low vision with no assistance, magnification software users, and users with cognitive problems, the layout begins to cause problems in focus order and reading order.
  irc (60 lines):
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: by default if you have grid lanes it will read out items in source order
    | <sgill> ... same as other display modes
    | <sgill> ... not ideal like every other display mode
    | <sgill> ... have reading-flow to help
    | <sgill> ... probably want something for grid lanes
    | <sgill> ... maybe non-controversial: lane-order
    | <sgill> ... useful if you are using grid lanes not for masonry use cases but flexible page layout cases where everything is explicitly placed
    | <sgill> .... happy to support this seems reasonable
    | <sgill> ... other one is the other axis standard masonry cases
    | <sgill> ... if you have just masonry placement it still doesn't always have a good reading order from source order
    | <sgill> ... like comics
    | <sgill> ... have a potential algorithm
    | <TabAtkins> https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/5675#issuecomment-4848519759
    | <sgill> ... first grab the first item not yet been read in every lane
    | <sgill> ... initial set we start from
    | <sgill> .... then see how much space it occupies (tall item dictates size of reading row)
    | <sgill> ... are the other lanes full of short items such that we can read them at once part of this region
    | <sgill> [talking about example from issue]
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: number of details to capture human heuristics
    | <sgill> .... don't need to go through all precise rules
    | <sgill> ... something like this is probably right though
    | <sgill> ... if no other ideas I suggest we add these two new reading order directions names TBD and precise details of cross lanes TBD
    | <miriam> I like this general approach
    | <florian> q+
    | <astearns> ack florian
    | <sgill> florian: think this ties back to one reason I have been mildly uncomfortable about reading-flow property
    | <sgill> ... keep adding values that only apply in certain modes
    | <sgill> ... feel like I want to reuse them when they can
    | <sgill> ... two values are they that different from what we have in grid?
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: yeah
    | <sgill> florian: having grid values do something on grid and not here and vice versa isn't great
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: very specifically went away from this direction with flex and grid even if we could have
    | <sgill> ... because details are different and we want to be clear you knew what you are doing for that layout mode
    | <sgill> ... wanted to be required to know what your container is doing
    | <sgill> ... also precise details mean having a really good name that is specific
    | <rachelandrew> +1 to Tab, I think this is really important to avoid reset stylesheet type things
    | <astearns> ack fantasai
    | <sgill> fantasai: +1 to florian we might want something like this for grid
    | <sgill> ... think it is interesting but quite experimental
    | <sgill> ... my suggestion is to add an appendix to the grid lanes spec
    | <sgill> ... saying we are drafting that out
    | <sgill> ... want to keep it in that spec since it is specific and will help with review/feedback
    | <alisonmaher> q+
    | <sgill> ... will help set expectations that it is not yet incorporated but working out the algorithm in that space is a good idea
    | <rachelandrew> q+
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: seems reasonable does it apply to per lane value too?
    | <sgill> fantasai: seems like it matches grid keyword
    | <sgill> ... general concerns about reading flow but don't think it adds to them
    | <astearns> ack alisonmaher
    | <sgill> alisonmaher: think this algorithm makes sense
    | <sgill> ... this is something we would like to have in our implementation
    | <sgill> ... what does experimental mean?
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: means you should be careful
    | <sgill> fantasai: at this point prototyping is a good plan and revisit if we ned to get past that
    | <kizu> q+
    | <astearns> ack rachelandrew
    | <sgill> rachelandrew: like the proposal
    | <sgill> ... in terms of developers this is the only thing I am hearing from them
    | <sgill> ... keep getting emails
    | <astearns> ack kizu

- 2026-08-05 #10884 [css-grid-3] -> [[masonry]]
  RESOLVED: Rename to 'fit-tolerance'.
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/10884#issuecomment-5192599929
  background: https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/9328#issuecomment-1823217994 didn't resolve on the name for this property.
  related: #9328 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/9328
  irc (40 lines):
    | <fantasai> See https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/10884#issuecomment-5073827734
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: This particular property we renamed several times. WebKit shipped with flow-tolerance, which is latest version.
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: We've seen some author confusion around this property
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: Maybe rename to a more intuitive name for what it is controlling
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: Confusion is that "flow" doesn't really explain what you're giving more slack to
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: It's about placement of a given item
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: And there's confusion with 'display: flow'
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: Proposal is to reconsider name and go with 'fit-tolerance'
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: This would also extend pretty nicely to Flexbox
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: Main pushback before on 'fit-tolerance' was confusion with 'object-fit', but I think that's less of a concern than confusion with 'flow'.
    | <TabAtkins> q+
    | <astearns> ack TabAtkins
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: Wanted to ask if we should change, and if WebKit would be open to change.
    | <SebastianZ> I'm torn between `placement-tolerance` and `fit-tolerance`.
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: Agree that 'flow-tolerance' is more confusing, and that 'fit' in this context doesn't have an association with 'object-fit' so not an issue there.
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: i don't like 'placement' because this has nothing to do with the 'place-*' properties.
    | <florian> seems ok to me
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: my personal opinion is that this makes sense and is worth the rename
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: I don't have an official wk position
    | <TabAtkins> sgill: I feel like any choice will make people unhappy, including keeping the existing one
    | <TabAtkins> florian: well from a compat perspective?
    | <TabAtkins> sgill: sooner rather than later
    | <miriam> q+
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: It's not very deployed at this point, so unlikely to be much dependency
    | <astearns> ack miriam
    | <fantasai> sammy: Maybe confusion with repeat(auto-fit/auto-fill)?
    | <fantasai> fantasai: Seems not too confusing
    | <fantasai> miriam: The question I got with my team was whether it should be more closely tied to grid-lanes
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: Still want to make it apply to Flexbox.
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: But need to do that soon, so that we don't get compat issues.
    | <fantasai> fantasai: Use case for flexbox is quite interesting, we should pursue it.
    | <fantasai> sammy: I'm surprised placement-tolerance is not more popular? Seems more to do with placement.
    | <fantasai> florian: I like to too, but it's quite long
    | <fantasai> astearns: Sounds like 'fit-tolerance' is one that has the most oomph behind it?
    | <fantasai> sammy: maybe we come back to this later?
    | <fantasai> bramus: Spec says "Placement Precision"
    | <fantasai> fantasai: I wanted a section title that would make sense no matter what we named the property
    | <fantasai> astearns: I propose we resolve on 'fit-tolerance' and let the Internet tell us if we're wrong. :)
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Rename to 'fit-tolerance'.
    | <fantasai> ACTION: Implement this quickly, and follow-up on Flexbox quickly.
- 2026-08-05 #11506 [css-box-4]
  RESOLVED: margin-trim doesn't affect the resolved value of margin properties
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/11506#issuecomment-5190507939
  background: While discussing https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/11325#issuecomment-2593588438 is was mentioned that margin-trim shouldn't affect the getComputedStyle of margins.
  related: #11325 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/11325
  irc (23 lines):
    | <emilio> fantasai: Ian opened this that margin-trim shouldn't affect getComputedStyle but some WPTs assert this behavior
    | <emilio> ... I think I agree with his proposal
    | <emilio> florian: this is gCS only, right not real computed styles right?
    | <emilio> fantasai: yes
    | <JohnJansen> q+
    | <emilio> ... I guess the idea is that it'd return the value ignoring margin-trim
    | <astearns> ack JohnJansen
    | <emilio> JohnJansen: curious, we all fail all of them
    | <emilio> s/them/the tests
    | <emilio> ... Do you know if they will make them pass?
    | <emilio> fantasai: the tests need to be fixed
    | <emilio> kbabbitt: so the answer is both
    | <emilio> PROPOSED: margin-trim doesn't affect the resolved value of margin properties
    | <emilio> RESOLVED: margin-trim doesn't affect the resolved value of margin properties
    | <emilio> florian: just to be clear this goes into cssom?
    | <fantasai> scribe+
    | <fantasai> emilio: I think CSSOM currently defines margins return the used value
    | <fantasai> florian: approximately yes, but it's pretty vague
    | <emilio> florian: seems we need to be a bit more specific
    | <emilio> fantasai: need to figure out what we do for margin-collapse
    | <emilio> florian: as long as we have agreement
    | <emilio> fantasai: probably should move to the css-box where margin is defined
    | <fantasai> ACTION: fantasai to edit
- 2026-08-05 #12023 [css-grid-3] -> [[masonry]]
  RESOLVED: Both grid and any grid-lanes shorthand we introduce reset all of the container properties for both grid and grid-lanes.
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/12023#issuecomment-5192928952
  background: Opening this issue to split out discussion from https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/11243#issuecomment-2584991716 on adjustments to shorthanding: - Any adjustments we might need to make the `grid*` shorthands accommodate masonry features. - Possible addition of a masonry-specific shorthand.
  related: #11243 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/11243, #11480 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/11480
  irc (45 lines):
    | <sgill> scribe+
    | <fantasai> s/Topic/Subtopic
    | <sgill> fantasai: been some discussion about adding shorthand and new syntax to existing for grid lanes
    | <sgill> ... lots been up in the air because of debating grid lanes direction and auto flow stuff
    | <sgill> ... alisonmaher and I could resolve on which properties get reset by shorthands
    | <sgill> ... every property reset by the grid shorthand + any possible grid lanes flow / management properties get reset either grid shorthand and new shorthand
    | <sgill> astearns: can you go into why
    | <TabAtkins> q+
    | <sgill> fantasai: grid shorthand is grid by itself so resets that
    | <sgill> ... if we introduce a grid-lanes shorthand it functionally sets up rows or columns and secondary axis is automatic placement of some kind
    | <sgill> ... that is true whether you are in grid or grid-lanes layout
    | <astearns> q+
    | <sgill> ... can have type of layout where you have explicit tracks and you flow down the tracks
    | <sgill> ... only difference is whether you align along the rows of the secondary axis
    | <sgill> ... if we create the shorthand for grid-lanes it will already reset a bunch of grid properties
    | <sgill> ... might as well reset all of them so you actually get that layout
    | <sgill> ... display property becomes a switch on whether you create secondary tracks or not
    | <sgill> ... it is a really common pattern to want to do that in grid as well
    | <astearns> ack TabAtkins
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: while I don't think it's important to support between grid and grid lanes I think it's useful to have both the shorthands reset the same set of things
    | <sgill> ... we don't have much other examples of these shorthands resetting multiple things
    | <alisonmaher> q+
    | <sgill> ... potentially confusing have substantial overlap resetting both but not all
    | <sgill> astearns: iiuc the developer would you the display property switch and nothing else
    | <sgill> ... because if you are switching using the grid lanes shorthand you might be resetting things that the grid may want to use but grid lanes ignores
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: that is ok because the style rules setting up the grid styles would just return to applying when switch
    | <sgill> ... only one would apply at a time or one is overriding
    | <sgill> ... when you stop doing the style the base styles would still be in effect
    | <sgill> ... setting a shorthand isn't like assigning a variable
    | <astearns> ack alisonmaher
    | <astearns> ack astearns
    | <sgill> alisonmaher: reasonable to reset properties for both
    | <sgill> ... can we resolve on that we want a grid lanes shorthand with shape TBD
    | <sgill> fantasai: think we need syntax before we can resolve
    | <sgill> alisonmaher: in either case we want a grid lanes shorthand
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: still the case the grid shorthand is bad to use for grid lanes
    | <sgill> .... going to still need the new shorthand
    | <sgill> fantasai: ok with pursuing proposals for it
    | <sgill> ... working through syntax is going to depend on other stuff but whether or not we have it is not
    | <sgill> astearns: not convinced but mostly I don't care since it is a shorthand '
    | <sgill> PROPOSED: pursue a grid-lanes shorthand and both the grid and grid-lanes shorthand would reset all of the properties for both grid and grid-lanes. syntax TBD
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Both grid and any grid-lanes shorthand we introduce reset all of the container properties for both grid and grid-lanes.
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Both grid and any grid-lanes shorthand we introduce reset all of the container properties for both grid and grid-lanes.
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Pursue a grid-lanes shorthand syntax.
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Pursue a grid-lanes shorthand syntax.

- 2026-08-05 #12971 [css-grid-3] -> [[masonry]]
  RESOLVED: reversing placement does not affect line track ordering or explicit placement
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/12971#issuecomment-5193128279
  background: From @celestepan's https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/12803#issuecomment-3387605000
  related: #3622 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/3622, #12803 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/12803
  irc (60 lines):
    | <sgill> cepan: track reversing in grid lanes reverses the track order we palce
    | <sgill> ... columns usually place left to right but with reverse we go right to left
    | <TabAtkins> q+
    | <sgill> ... should we reverse lines and numbering of lines also
    | <sgill> ... if 1 is on the left with reverse would it be on the right
    | <sgill> ... I propose that we should not because if authors wanted to they could use writing direction
    | <astearns> ack TabAtkins
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: agree. in general making too much of the coordinate stuff relative to flow direction like in flex was a mistake
    | <sgill> ... easy to think about when ordering and numbering is consistent regardless of flow of things
    | <sgill> ... line 1 should always be start most
    | <astearns> ack fantasai
    | <sgill> fantasai: 1.) using writing mode and direction is not flow control
    | <sgill> ... should only use when wanting to modify text
    | <sgill> ... 2.) we have numbering and naming and whether grid-column-start is on left or right side
    | <sgill> ... I think whether we flip the grid or not the grid-column-start and 1 side should correspond to same side
    | <sgill> ... we if want to flip the grid the grid column start side should also
    | <sgill> ... less certain that the right decision is not to flip but don't actually know
    | <astearns> q+
    | <sgill> ... want others to weigh in
    | <alisonmaher> q+
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: if we flip them we create a grid-start and grid-end set of directions
    | <sgill> ... confusing with flexbox
    | <sgill> ... should not imply or introduce grid-start and grid-end set
    | <sgill> fantasai: think you might want to do that for the alignment properties
    | <sgill> ... when you reverse the stacking of the items you probably want to have the alignment start on that side
    | <sgill> ... think we going to need a set of flow directions for that
    | <cepan> q+
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: can we spell the flow relative starts flex-start and flex-end?
    | <sgill> alisonmaher: I think in the spec it says what happens if you use them outside flex
    | <sgill> fantasai: want to hear from people who use grid
    | <TabAtkins> i retract, my thought was about the weird flex-start fallbacks we have, but those are for back-compat, not really on purpose
    | <sgill> astearns: wanted to also note the differentiation between the grid start and alignment start stuff
    | <astearns> ack astearns
    | <astearns> ack alisonmaher
    | <oriol> This is getting into https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14052
    | <sgill> alisonmaher: if we do allow this to reverse what happens if the writing mode swaps the order and you also reverse
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: everything in the spec is relative to writing mode direction
    | <astearns> ack cepan
    | <sgill> cepan: with flipping start edge I think we had an issue for that
    | <sgill> ... I think we resolved on saying no
    | <sgill> ... wanted to say maybe it could be separate. having track reverse does one thing but separate set of things could flip the properties of the box
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: probably reasonable to define flow start and flow end like flex
    | <sgill> fantasai: if we do that it should be an alias to the flex ones
    | <miriam> q+
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: for the rest of this we should stick for the writing mode direction
    | <astearns> ack miriam
    | <sgill> Miriam: agree with what tab said
    | <alisonmaher> q+
    | <sgill> ... don't think default ones should change
    | <sgill> ... haven't looked into use cases to know if we need flow relative ones though
    | <astearns> ack alisonmaher
    | <sgill> alisonmaher: if we were creating the flow relative would it apply to flex and grid lanes or more
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: haven't done reversals in grid yet but they are in grid 3
    | <sgill> fantasai: been planned for years
    | <miriam> whatever we do should certainly match grid
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: we are depending on flex start turning into start on grid
    | <sgill> ... actually doesn't matter what we do for grid since we don't have reversals yet
    | <sgill> ... seems like alignment stuff might need to correspond to flow direction
    | <sgill> astearns: are we converging on reversing does not affect line track ordering or start and end?
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: yes and should also open issues for the flow relative stuff
- 2026-08-05 #13260 [css-sizing-4]
  RESOLVED: Resolve the margin behavior against the same containing block that we resolve percentages against
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13260#issuecomment-5190789559
  background: https://drafts.csswg.org/css-sizing-4/#stretch-fit-sizing
  related: #11044 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/11044
  irc (41 lines):
    | <emilio> astearns: ian does let's do what servo does
    | <emilio> oriol: I'd like something else
    | <emilio> ... some context
    | <emilio> ... if you have a block level element that's stretching, that doesn't look good if margins collapse
    | <emilio> ... we resolved that if the margins would collapse based on some heuristics
    | <emilio> ... then we'd treat the margins as 0 for stretch
    | <emilio> ... it's not exactly checking for collapsing
    | <emilio> ... it doesn't check what happens if the block is inside an inline
    | <emilio> ... so blink has an anonymous block for this and blink thinks always the margins will collapse
    | <emilio> ... so element stretches to a bigger size that then overflows
    | <emilio> ... because the element doesn't stretch against the anon parent but the containing block
    | <emilio> ... you can check the issue for test-cases
    | <emilio> ... left is using borders which prevent margin-collapsing, right outline which doesn't
    | <emilio> ... as you can see in the border case blink overflows
    | <emilio> ... what I did in servo was to always consider margins
    | <emilio> ... so never treat as zero
    | <emilio> ... but if you're using outlines it doesn't fill the block
    | <emilio> ... I think the servo behavior makes more sense than Blink's but I don't think it's ideal
    | <emilio> ... second row should ideally look the same as the first
    | <emilio> ... this would mean that instead of checking the parent we'd check the containing block
    | <TabAtkins> Q+
    | <emilio> ... there's the question for the inline levels whether they have borders or that
    | <emilio> ... I think it's fine not to check them
    | <emilio> ... the inline elements would end up fragmented before / after / around the block levels
    | <emilio> ... the fragments around won't get padding / border
    | <emilio> ... but in the fragment tree they're effectively uncles
    | <emilio> ... so if we're ignoring siblings which we resolved already
    | <emilio> ... seems fine to ignore uncles too
    | <emilio> ... proposed resolution would be option 1 in the issue
    | <emilio> ... it'd change the spec to say the conditions would be checked on the containing block rather than the parent box
    | <emilio> ... which is what the element is stretching against
    | <astearns> ack fantasai
    | <emilio> fantasai: which is the same thing we resolve percentages around right?
    | <astearns> ack TabAtkins
    | <emilio> ... I agree, we should resolve this against the same element
    | <emilio> ... that we resolve percents against
    | <emilio> TabAtkins: I agree, I think the parent wording might've been a mistake
    | <emilio> PROPOSED: Check the containing-block instead of the parent
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Resolve the margin behavior against the same containing block that we resolve percentages against
    | <emilio> +1
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Resolve the margin behavior against the same containing block that we resolve percentages against
- 2026-08-05 #13731 [css-box-4]
  RESOLVED: margin-trim only applies to block / multicol
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13731#issuecomment-5190616401
  background: @tabatkins , @mstensho and myself have had some discussions about applying margin-trim to flex/grid.
  related: #3255 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/3255
  irc (12 lines):
    | <emilio> TabAtkins: ian points out that a while ago we resolved to allow margin-trim on grid / flex
    | <emilio> ... use case was about gaps
    | <emilio> ... this predates gaps
    | <emilio> ... original author confirmed their use case was about that
    | <emilio> ... implementation is complex
    | <emilio> ... so it'd be nice to revert the previous resolution so margin-trim only applies to block / multicol
    | <emilio> ... and thus only in the block axis
    | <emilio> +1
    | <rachelandrew> +1
    | <fantasai> +1 from WebKit
    | <emilio> PROPOSED: margin-trim only applies to block / multicol
    | <emilio> RESOLVED: margin-trim only applies to block / multicol
- 2026-08-05 #13834 [css-box-4,css-inline-3]
  RESOLVED: Apply margin-break to inline-axis margins of inline-level boxes (after white space collapsing). The initial value behaves as 'keep' for compat. End margin hangs if subsequent spaces are hanging.
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13834#issuecomment-5190596494
  background: I wanted to pull out [this comment](https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/3216#issuecomment-2462045649) from @tats-u
  related: #3216 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/3216
  irc (60 lines):
    | <emilio> fantasai: in the test-case there's a button but there's no margin except when it's not at the beginning of the inline axis
    | <emilio> ... I think it's the same as what happens in the block axis based on margin-break
    | <emilio> ... for fragmentation
    | <florian> q+
    | <emilio> ... so I propose to reuse margin-break but apply it to inline margins on inline boxes too
    | <emilio> seems fine
    | <emilio> TabAtkins: I don't think this use case is used by margins with or without margin-trim
    | <emilio> fantasai: margin-break, not margin-trim
    | <emilio> ... that has auto | keep | discard
    | <emilio> ... if it lands on a fragmentation break then those get cut
    | <emilio> ... if you set discard
    | <emilio> ... auto will keep or discard depending whether it's a forced break
    | <emilio> TabAtkins: to be clear, bottom margin won't be shifted to the next column
    | <emilio> ... right?
    | <emilio> fantasai: that's basically the behavior requested except in the inline axis
    | <emilio> ... so I think we should reuse this property
    | <emilio> TabAtkins: seems plausible
    | <astearns> ack florian
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Apply margin-break to inline-axis margins of inline-level boxes
    | <emilio> florian: So it'd apply at the start of the line
    | <emilio> ... however you got here or only after a force break
    | <alisonmaher> q+
    | <emilio> fantasai: given we have an auto value, we might need to define auto to be keep for inlines
    | <emilio> ... if we want the auto behavior we might want a separate keyword
    | <emilio> ... discard applies to both kinds of breaks
    | <emilio> florian: the other question is that this needs to behave properly with white-space collapsing
    | <emilio> ... we need to discard margins after white-space collapsing
    | <emilio> fantasai: agreed
    | <astearns> ack alisonmaher
    | <emilio> alisonmaher: would this work on the first line or only after a break?
    | <emilio> fantasai: both
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Apply margin-break to inline-axis margins of inline-level boxes (after white space collapsing). The initial value behaves as 'keep' for compat.
    | <emilio> florian: support, but a nuance, what about empty boxes?
    | <emilio> fantasai: I think I'm happy to say that that's not at the beginning of the line
    | <sgill> q+
    | <emilio> florian: if you have empty spans in the line
    | <fantasai> florian: We have precedent to ignore them
    | <emilio> TabAtkins: for block margins it ignores
    | <emilio> fantasai: but margins on block collapse
    | <emilio> florian: I think we should skip empty boxes
    | <emilio> oriol: also abspos
    | <emilio> fantasai: yes
    | <astearns> ack sgill
    | <emilio> sgill: this is supposed to remove margins at the end of the line right?
    | <emilio> fantasai: yes
    | <emilio> sgill: but can you then fit more stuff after the margin?
    | <emilio> fantasai: no, this should happen after breaking
    | <emilio> florian: that's a more interesting case
    | <emilio> fantasai: it can influence breaking in the sense that you could keep the button on the line if it'd fit without the margin but it can't fit more stuff
    | <emilio> astearns: is that well defined?
    | <emilio> emilio: if you pull more stuff it wouldn't be at the end
    | <emilio> florian: it might fall out from the definition
    | <emilio> ... at the end of the line it's interesting, because whitespace can hang
    | <emilio> ... would make a difference for justification
    | <emilio> ... if the other thing after yourself is empty elements or discarded / hanging spaces
    | <emilio> fantasai: then the margin is allowed to hang
    | <emilio> ... if spaces are hanging then the margin should hang
    | <emilio> florian: I think we don't need to precisely resolve on this, we can review the text once it's there
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Apply margin-break to inline-axis margins of inline-level boxes (after white space collapsing). The initial value behaves as 'keep' for compat. End margin hangs if subsequent spaces are hanging.
    | <florian> +1
- 2026-08-05 #13930 [css-grid-1,css-grid-2]
  RESOLVED: when grid-placement is fully auto in an axis, use the scrollport edges (otherwise do as specced)
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13930#issuecomment-5193782098
  background: Consider the following:
  irc (33 lines):
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: weird case with abspos and overflowing grids
    | <sgill> ... spec says one thing that is good that implementations never followed
    | <sgill> ... Ian wants to change and thinks he can
    | <sgill> ... want to make sure this is generally good with everyone else
    | <sgill> ... grid container 100x100
    | <sgill> ... its grid is 150x150
    | <sgill> ... abspos child with grid container as containing block
    | <sgill> ... if the grid placement properties on the abspos are auto then we take the container's padding edge
    | <sgill> ... if specific grid line we use that line
    | <sgill> ... parenthetical saying instead of using grid container's padding edge we use scrollable area's padding edge
    | <sgill> fantasai: assuming that means scrollable content area
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: behavior we want is if your abspos is using g grid lines do that
    | <sgill> ... if it is double auto in that axis act like normal abspos (padding edge of container)
    | <sgill> ... if grid line on one edge and auto other then you scrollable area
    | <sgill> ... current browser behavior is if you grid-row-start: 1 and end auto we put start edge on one grid line and end on the padding edge of the container
    | <astearns> ack fantasai
    | <sgill> fantasai: good problem statement
    | <sgill> ... think we should take the outer most of the padding edge vs the grid start line or end line
    | <sgill> ... basically the grid itself pushes out the effective padding edge
    | <sgill> ... things would continue to work as they do now unless they overflow in which we anchor to the appropriate edge
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: think it is ok but want to double check with Ian
    | <sgill> TabAtkins: think it is ok what fantasai said actually
    | <sgill> astearns: wish Emilio was here to see if it was ok to change
    | <TabAtkins> dholbert:
    | <TabAtkins> ping
    | <emilio> hm?
    | <sgill> fantasai: ok I think I see the potential compat issue
    | <sgill> ... seems ok though
    | <emilio> So proposal is using the bigger of grid area vs padding area?
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: when grid-placement is fully auto in an axis, use the scrollport edges (otherwise do as specced)
    | <astearns> depending on whether things are using auto or not
    | <emilio> that seems fine iiuc
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: when grid-placement is fully auto in an axis, use the scrollport edges (otherwise do as specced)
- 2026-08-05 #13950 [css-grid-3] -> [[masonry]]
  RESOLVED: Priority of stretch alignment vs sizing properties is same as in flex/grid
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13950#issuecomment-5192423208
  background: CSS Grid and CSS Flex both ignore `align-self: stretch` and `align-items: stretch` when an item has a specified size. Part of our motivations in this discussion: https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/10275#issuecomment-3666150085 were to fill out the masonry layout visually, avoiding empty bottoms.
  related: #10275 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/10275
  irc (20 lines):
    | <fantasai> cepan: One of the values we can use for stacking-axis alignment is 'stretch'. This stretches to fill available space around it.
    | <fantasai> cepan: if author has set explicit size for item, should we stretch it?
    | <fantasai> cepan: In flex/grid, stretch does not override preferred size
    | <fantasai> cepan: fantasai said we could use min-height if wanting to stretch
    | <fantasai> cepan: do we also honor max-height?
    | <fantasai> cepan: I propose we do, because if explicitly setting height they probably want that height
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: Agreed
    | <fantasai> fantasai: We should match flex/grid in this respect
    | <alisonmaher> +1
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Priority of stretch alignment vs sizing properties is same as in flex/grid
    | <fantasai> astearns: So strecth has no effect on explicit sizes?
    | <fantasai> Several: Correct.
    | <fantasai> cepan: Comment suggests adding a keyword to have something like 'strong stretch'
    | <fantasai> cepan: This would allows overriding the explicit sizes
    | <fantasai> cepan: Should we add that? Or open new issue?
    | <fantasai> astearns: Separate issue
    | <fantasai> astearns: We have to think what it would do in all contexts it could be applied to
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Priority of stretch alignment vs sizing properties is same as in flex/grid
    | <fantasai> astearns: I'll leave it up to you, cepan, if there's a strong enough use case for the new keyword.
    | <fantasai> s/keyword/keyword to motivate opening an issue.

  RESOLVED: Remove issue, leave spec as-is (not updating auto-placement cursor with explicit placement)
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13987#issuecomment-5192487721
  background: In the spec, there is already an issue regarding whether or not we should update the auto-placement cursor after explicitly placing an item: Link: https://drafts.csswg.org/css-grid-3/#grid-lanes-layout-algorithm <img width="500" height="307.56" alt="Image" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/839f6bea-71d4-4115-b8ec-1ae75ebca8bc" />
  irc (27 lines):
    | <fantasai> cepan: There's a question in the spec about whether the auto-placement cursor when explicitly placing an item
    | <fantasai> cepan: But after seeing Tab's comment, I think I agree we shouldn't update it.
    | <sgill> q+
    | <fantasai> cepan: Compare to dense packing, we don't update the cursor there iether
    | <fantasai> s/iether/either/
    | <fantasai> astearns: So proposal is to just remove the issue from the spec?
    | <fantasai> fantasai: Yes
    | <astearns> ack sgill
    | <fantasai> Sammy: would this be a behavior change compared to normal?
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: In normal Grid, you do all explicit placement first. And then go back and fill in auto-placed items.
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: In Grid Lanes, they are placed interleaved
    | <fantasai> Sammy: Even for item in assigned column?
    | <fantasai> fantasai: Yes, because more constrained, we assign it first to ensure it gets what it needs
    | <fantasai> fantasai: Auto-placement backfills whatever slots are left.
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: [summarizes spec]
    | <alisonmaher> q+
    | <astearns> ack alisonmaher
    | <fantasai> fantasai: So backfilling here would also be consistent
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: Either way seems reasonable.
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: For a11y, is it better to go closer to DOM order, or better to go next?
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: I think they're both potentially confusing.
    | <TabAtkins> here's the grid layout rules https://drafts.csswg.org/css-grid-2/#auto-placement-algo
    | <fantasai> astearns: I think I agree with the concern that it's maybe slightly worse for a11y, but not so much worse than regular grid lanes placement that it needs special-casing.
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Remove issue, leave spec as-is (not updating auto-placement cursor with explicit placement)
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Remove issue, leave spec as-is (not updating auto-placement cursor with explicit placement)
    | <TabAtkins> diff between 1-3-4-5-2-6 and 1-4-5-6-2-3 (assume 2 is explicitly placed in second-to-last)
    | <TabAtkins> both potentially confusing in similar ways

  RESOLVED: Once the UA is confident that the scroll operation has stopped, MUST ensure the layout is consistent with a full relayout at that point. UA MAY relayout during scroll; and if not relaying out, then MUST slide the anchorpos with the scroll operation as currently specified.
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14112#issuecomment-5189681671
  background: I recently [created a demo of the 'ideal' sizing for custom select](https://jakearchibald.com/2026/goldilocks-select-height/#putting-it-all-together), and noticed that how anchor positioning reacts to scrolling is inconsistent across browsers and platforms.

    | <bramus> … that parts differs per browser
    | <bramus> … sometimes a hover in safari, in chrome more changes have to happen, etc
    | <bramus> … looking for agreement that this is not great
    | <bramus> … closest to a proposal is that on scroll end, things should be recalced
    | <emilio> q+
    | <bramus> … because implmeenters are not keen to do it during scroll
    | <bramus> … with scroll end, we’d at least have sth consistent

    | <SebastianZ> q+
    | <astearns> q+
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: it is unfortunate that ian is not here (tz)
    | <bramus> … as I don’t understand what chrome is doing
    | <bramus> … doing it on scroll is a layout change and should be ruled out
    | <bramus> fantasai: happens because we do a new fallback cycle
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: spec says to skip the fallback styles and only test other ones if they would fit
    | <bramus> … everybody seems to be violating spec

    | <bramus> … <missed> feels and looks stupid
    | <bramus> … authors want to replace their JS lib with anchor pos, but the thing shrinking and staying small prevents them
    | <bramus> … things should remain same size, or be a lot more agressive to relayout on scroll so that is does not look asymmetrical
    | <kizu> q+
    | <bramus> JakeA: So you are saying to go with the scrollend thing, or to not make it smaller?
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: per spec, it should check the other overflow style to see if it fits better

    | <astearns> ack emilio
