- 2026-08-05 #14153 [css-gaps-1,css-grid-3] -> [[masonry]]
  RESOLVED: each item generates a rule for itself start-wise in the stacking direction
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14153#issuecomment-5191855003
  background: - The normative definition of a **gap** per container type covers only multicol, flex, and grid containers. **There is no grid lanes entry.** - The **Applies to** line of `row-gap` / `column-gap` (css-gaps-1 `Row and Column Gutters` section) likewise omits grid lanes containers.
  irc (47 lines):
    | <fantasai> javierct: Seems like this is mostly editorial catch-up, but there's one issue to be aware of
    | <fantasai> javierct: What we're proposing is that the gutter is after every item, and each item owns the gap of the item above them.
    | <fantasai> javierct: The diagram below is wrong. We have that big gap, proposed to be owned by the spaner
    | <astearns> q+
    | <astearns> ack alisonmaher
    | <fantasai> javierct: We're just proposing that for any given item, if there's a gap above it, then it owns the gap
    | <fantasai> javierct: Interesting case is in this case: https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14153#issuecomment-5133521003
    | <fantasai> javierct: we have a weird behavior where we if we have a larger spanner on top of a smaller item, and longer item
    | <fantasai> javierct: we get a case where it's not treated the same
    | <TabAtkins> q+
    | <fantasai> javierct: nothing interesting in grid axis, but stacking axis is different
    | <astearns> ack TabAtkins
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: Is this what we do for grids?
    | <fantasai> javierct: For grids it's different because gaps in grid are run across a whole container. But here we only have per-track gaps
    | <fantasai> javierct: So spanners don't really play into this.
    | <fantasai> javierct: in this case it's different because we want to define gaps per items
    | <fantasai> javierct: In stacking direction we tie to the item rather than the "track" (that doesn't exist)
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: If #﻿1 was span 3 and slightly taller item in 4th track, thing would have to go somewhere else
    | <fantasai> astearns: Diagrams in the original comment are slightly wrong in that the first diagram would have that topmost red gap split between items 4 & 3
    | <fantasai> astearns: and again for 2nd diagram
    | <fantasai> astearns: lowest re gap would be split between 5 & 6
    | <astearns> ack astearns
    | <astearns> ack fantasai
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: I think we should decide whether we want the gaps to be always split, or want spanners to always have a continuous rule
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: I think continuous is desired and we should use that consistently on both sides
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: that would get you exatly the diagram on the top
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: on the bottom, the length of the deco would be span-4 for both of them
    | <alisonmaher> q+
    | <TabAtkins> javierct: that's what we initially wanted, which is why the first commente's diagrams are like that
    | <TabAtkins> javierct: but it's very ambiguous once we have... say in the second diagram, on both sides of item 2 we had long spanners
    | <TabAtkins> javierct: say next to item 2, we had spanner flanking it on both sides that were long
    | <TabAtkins> javierct: it's ambiguous which gaps...
    | <TabAtkins> javierct: item 1 is a long spanners, we'd have the rule going all across
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: what are those new items spanning?
    | <TabAtkins> javierct: so pretending...
    | <TabAtkins> alisonmaher: I have a simpler example
    | <astearns> ack alisonmaher
    | <TabAtkins> alisonmaher: looking at last diagram again
    | <TabAtkins> alisonmaher: item 1 was actually span 2/span 2
    | <TabAtkins> alisonmaher: then item 2 is overlapping them
    | <TabAtkins> alisonmaher: who gets which rule?
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: ah I see, especially if one of the split item1 block was a bit shorter...
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: so the gap we're associating here is the one on the placement-start side, in the stacking direction
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: okay I think we can go with this for now
    | <TabAtkins> alisonmaher: and I think these cases are uncommon, so we can probably change if we have a better idea later
    | <TabAtkins> proposed: each item generates a rule for itself start-wise in the stacking direction
    | <TabAtkins> RESOLVED: each item generates a rule for itself start-wise in the stacking direction
- 2026-08-05 #14154 [css-gaps-1]
  RESOLVED: Gap is exactly the gap that is specified, not including space left over due to placement (which could in theory be used for dense packing or alignment). The gap is adjacent to the spanner (it does not drift into this space).
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14154#issuecomment-5191730059
  background: Along the stacking axis, a grid lanes container can contain empty space that is *not* an authored gutter.
  irc (8 lines):
    | <fantasai> javierct: What the title says.
    | <alisonmaher> q+
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Gap is exactly the gap that is specified, not including space left over due to placement (which could in theory be used for dense packing or alignment). The gap is adacent to the spanner (it does not drift into this space).
    | <fantasai> s/adacent/adjacent/
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: We want decorations adjacent to spanner to connect
    | <kbabbitt> +1
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: so we should go with this proposal
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Gap is exactly the gap that is specified, not including space left over due to placement (which could in theory be used for dense packing or alignment). The gap is adjacent to the spanner (it does not drift into this space).

- 2026-08-05 #14156 [css-gaps-1]
  RESOLVED: Colors/styles/etc are assigned independently per track, from placement start.
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14156#issuecomment-5192334195
  background: The gap-decoration properties accept value lists, including `repeat( <n>, … )` and `repeat( auto, … )`. For grid and multicol, values are assigned to gaps in a well-defined order along each axis. For grid lanes containers, the assignment order along the **stacking axis** is undefined, because that axis has no shared lines/tracks and its gutters are misaligned across lanes.
  irc (60 lines):
    | <fantasai> javierct: this is how we assign lists of values
    | <fantasai> javierct: For gird axis, do same as grid containers -- assign values sequentiall
    | <fantasai> javierct: In the stacking axis, what we propose is assigning values in lane by lane order, with each lane independent
    | <fantasai> javierct: Example we have red, orange, green, blue
    | <fantasai> javierct: each lane starts with red, and continues from there
    | <TabAtkins> q+
    | <florian> q+
    | <fantasai> javierct: the spanner gap however occupies a single one
    | <astearns> ack TabAtkins
    | <fantasai> javierct: so all of it will be red, even if in 2n column it would be second (not first) rule
    | <fantasai> [missed]
    | <fantasai> javierct: We're saying that the spanner's gaps is belonging to the middle gap
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: If two spanners, B3 would be green but C3 would be orange.
    | <fantasai> Example: Add item C2.5 above the spanner. Would above C3 be green or orange?
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: I think spanner should reset it
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: imagine spanner as the first item across the top
    | <fantasai> javierct: so we do take into account, above the spanner we have just one gap, we still consider it two gaps for assignment purposes so in third track ...
    | <fantasai> javierct: So we're saying it would reset it?
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: Better way to say it, if spanner looks in startmost track, if something is below a spanner, it just looks at what the preceding rule was regardless of what item generated it
    | <florian> q?
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: so whatever color spanner uses, item following it would use the next color
    | <fantasai> javierct: I don't feel very strongly  ... might be a little weird
    | <fantasai> javierct: As far as how we define this, wouldn't suffice to say that we do this per lane independently. If we define that way then what we suggest here is strange. So we would need to define it some other way
    | <fantasai> alisonmaher: Have gaps in different tracks, but owned by same item.
    | <astearns> ack florian
    | <fantasai> florian: In terms of what happens after spanner, I agree with what was proposed
    | <fantasai> florian: But going in other direction, for decoration above the spanner, consider both tracks
    | <fantasai> florian: Take the max + 1
    | <alisonmaher> q+
    | <fantasai> florian: In this case would be orange, because there's at least one red above it
    | <fantasai> florian: Downside of current is that it repeats. With this idea you'd skip a red and you'd be thing after red
    | <alisonmaher> q-
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: In other cases you'll get repetition. No matter what it can be possible.
    | <fantasai> florian: Impossible to be perfect.
    | <alisonmaher> q+
    | <astearns> ack fantasai
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: I think thinking about this has got me rethinking the previous issue
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: I think we're creating a problem for ourselves if we're tracking across columns
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: when you're painting stuff, if it's per column it's easy, just do it in order
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: but if you're leaking info from one column into another, you have to keep track kof the association between an individual spanner and its color
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: that requires tracking more info and it's harder to do
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: I don't think people will be that excited about this specific behavior
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: so in the interest of making the behavior simpler, I think it would be better if the gaps were fully independent
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: which would mean splitting the rule over spanners at all times
    | <astearns> qq+
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: that would also give us consistency for rules before/after a spanner, which we currently don't
    | <TabAtkins> javierct: so even if you did rule-break:none, the spanner's rule won't connect?
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: yes. if you want a connected line you have to do the same thing for the top half that you'd use for the top half, which is use a border
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: this way you'll get consistent beahvior between the two cases, *and* have a simpler painting algo
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: the design consideration here is whether the gaps are part of the overall grid, in which case your solution can be reasonable, or if the grid-lanes gaps are really meant to be an item decoration, in which case one of the other proposals can be reasonable
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: i'm not entirely sure which is closer to author intent
    | <kbabbitt> q+
    | <astearns> ack astearns
    | <Zakim> astearns, you wanted to react to fantasai
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: the rules are specified on the container, so there's that
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: if the authors wants to associate the rule with the spanner, they probably want it symmetrical on both sides, but we're saying you dont' get that
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: so it's a weird inconsistency with rule beahvior depending on being above or below the spanner anyway, which feels uncomfortable
    | <astearns> ack alisonmaher
    | <TabAtkins> alisonmaher: I was going to say something similar to Alan
    | <TabAtkins> alisonmaher: my vision is for a spanner an author *would* want things connected...
- 2026-08-05 #14156 [css-gaps-1]
  RESOLVED: In flex, grid, and grid lanes, assignment is in placement direction (which may not be writing direction, if reversed)
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14156#issuecomment-5192334195
  background: The gap-decoration properties accept value lists, including `repeat( <n>, … )` and `repeat( auto, … )`. For grid and multicol, values are assigned to gaps in a well-defined order along each axis. For grid lanes containers, the assignment order along the **stacking axis** is undefined, because that axis has no shared lines/tracks and its gutters are misaligned across lanes.
  irc (60 lines):

- 2026-08-05 #14169 [css-gaps-1]
  RESOLVED: Grid axis rules match Grid
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14169#issuecomment-5192371025
  background: The `normal` initial value is defined per container type: for **grid**, a decoration starts and ends at "T" intersections (it stops when it meets an item spanning across the gap) but continues through "cross" intersections; for **flex**, `normal` behaves like `none` (each line's decorations are continuous and independent). Grid lanes containers are not yet covered.
  irc (13 lines):
    | <fantasai> javierct: Trivial given previous resolutions@
    | <fantasai> javierct: Want to define rule-break: normal for grid lanes
    | <fantasai> javierct: In grid lanes, propose to match grid
    | <fantasai> javierct: And for normal, that means stop at spanners to not paint through them.
    | <fantasai> javierct: For stacking axis because they split, even with spanners, then it means none or intersection have no effect in the stacking axis
    | <fantasai> javierct: so normal will do the only thing available: paint the one small rule for the track
    | <kbabbitt> +1
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: agreed
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Grid axis rules match Grid
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Stacking axis rules are always split, per previous resolution.
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Grid axis rules match Grid
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Stacking axis rules are always split, per previous resolution.
    | <fantasai> fantasai: Thanks to MSFT folks to draw so many fantastic diagrams. Let's put them in the specs, too. :)

- 2026-08-05 #14190 [css-align-3]
  RESOLVED: Adopt overrideable UA stylesheet rules for centering contents of form controls via align-content per Emilio's comment, instead of UA magic.
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14190#issuecomment-5190728686
  background: https://drafts.csswg.org/css-align-3/#propdef-align-content https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/rendering.html#button-layout
  irc (56 lines):
    | <TabAtkins> scribe+
    | <TabAtkins> oriol: If you have a button element that's a block, the contents get centered vertically in block axis
    | <TabAtkins> oriol: I was thinking it would be possible to use align-content to change this. but no impl lets you; the impls force it to centering. The only way to fix this is to switch the button to flex or something
    | <TabAtkins> oriol: my proposal is to make it work
    | <TabAtkins> oriol: so proposal is have the UA stylesheet set align-content:center, then authors can change it
    | <TabAtkins> oriol: this has some effects; if the button is display:grid it'll center, where today it doesn't. it will only affect multiline flex buttons
    | <TabAtkins> oriol: Ian says it's fine
    | <emilio> https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14190#issuecomment-4995219911
    | <TabAtkins> oriol: there's also more than just buttons here, might be safe vs unsafe. details in the issue if you want to check
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: the main proposal is to implement the alignment of input/button/select using align-content
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: potential compat - I prototyped it, and only had to change one test that was testing grid ocntainers
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: this is effectively the beahvior we have for blocks
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: so it's just that it affects grid buttons
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: I think it's worth trying
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: we've done similar simplifications in the past for thingsl ike clipping and were able to get away with it
    | <kizu> +1
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: so i'd like to resolve on it and change the few WPTs that depend on it
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: if we run into compat issues we can revert at that point
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: this is a lot easier to explain to authors what's going on, the button centering has been magic for a long time
    | <astearns> ack fantasai
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: this sounds great
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: if it's compat we should do it
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: why are we excluding checkbox/radio?
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: I took from the same rule that... [looks at HTML spec]
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: those don't have content
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: they will, tho
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: ah yeah
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: but in HTML spec this is basically just the Selectors for text controls that currently center their content
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: I think check boxes with appearance:none do use block layout, so it might make the change a bit riskier
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: we can discuss puting those Selectors in, I can try and see if it breaks much
    | <astearns> q+
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: maybe it can use "safe center" so if it's not overflowing it wont' have much of an effect
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: so yeah i'd just like to avoid for now
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: I can add a pref with safe centering of checkbox/radio and see what happens
    | <astearns> ack astearns
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: my q was the extent of the change
    | <sgill> q+
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: are we saying we're going to have align-content apply to all block containers, and then figure out which controls need it?
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: it already applies to block containers. it just doesn't work here on buttons because we use c++ magic
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: buttons are safe-centered, inputs are unsafe-centered, what author says doens't matter
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: this just moves those behaviors into ua stylesheet instead
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: what implications for appearance:base?
    | <astearns> ack sgill
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: it should apply as well, the UA stylesheet isn't specific here
    | <TabAtkins> sgill: I think in WebKit by default the button is a flexbox
    | <TabAtkins> sgill: I think we'd have to modify for this one
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: WebKit probably has the anonymous box thing, button is a flexbox, you have an anonymous block, you center that block with flexbox alignment
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: we made the change a while back, gecko and blink, to not use flexbox+anonymous block. we just use a regular block now
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: so should be compatible
    | <TabAtkins> sgill: okay that's a good sign
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: so I think you will need to make that change back to block first, but then this change will fix alignment for you
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: proposed is to tweak the HTML stylesheet to implement button/input/select styling with UA styles rather than magic
    | <kbabbitt> +1
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Adopt overrideable UA stylesheet rules for centering via align-content per Emilio's comment, instead of UA magic.
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: objections? comments?
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Adopt overrideable UA stylesheet rules for centering contents of form controls via align-content per Emilio's comment, instead of UA magic.
- 2026-08-05 #14235 [css-link-params-1]
  RESOLVED: Prefix with :~: -- and write an explainer and send this over to the TAG.
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14235#issuecomment-5191610001
  background: The current spec defines link parameters with a bare fragment:
  irc (60 lines):
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: One of the ways you can pass a parameter in link-params spec is via URL fragment
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: I defined fragment parsing for this to be akin to what SVG does for its special fragment directives
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: But smfr pointed out that we should align with scroll-to-text-fragment, because that was intentionally designed to deal with compat and special interaction with target vs extra commands
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: That sounds fine to me
    | <kizu> +1
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: So proposal is to adopt the :~: syntax
    | <fantasai> astearns: Is this the first time you're advocating for more characters to type?
    | <SebastianZ> q+
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: I don't *always* advocate for less characters!
    | <astearns> ack SebastianZ
    | <fantasai> SebastianZ: Generally I agree that looks good
    | <fantasai> SebastianZ: But we are already using the just hash syntax elsewhere, e.g. cropping images we are not using it
    | <lea> q?
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: Media fragments pre-dates it
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: Probably should rebase to allow either way
    | <lea> q+
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: I think this would be the second use
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: But the intention was to do this going forward in fragments
    | <astearns> ack lea
    | <ChrisL> `q+
    | <fantasai> lea: Is it the :~: or function syntax?
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: That's the prefix, and then you define your special syntax
    | <TabAtkins> foo.svg#:~:param(--foo, red)&param(--bar, blue)
    | <fantasai> lea: I assumed this was to avoid clashes with author syntax, but :~: is to avoid clash
    | <fantasai> lea: So can we use a syntax that looks like assignment, instead of a function?
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: The variable names here are pretty open-ended, seems like a problem
    | <fantasai> lea: I suppose
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: [missed]
    | <fantasai> lea: It's kinda weird there, too
    | <fantasai> s/[missed]/I use param() function the other ways for passing parameters in CSS syntax/
    | <fantasai> lea: Why did we decide to use a function call for assignment there?
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: The first (and what I think is the most natural way to use these in CSS) was going to be as URL parameters
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: and all of those are shaped as functions, for parsing reasons
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: so copying that
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: It's also natural because it look similar to var with fallback, with arbitrary value after comma
    | <fantasai> lea: var() is not an assignment, it's getting a value
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: But shape of "specifier, fallback" is a common pattern
    | <fantasai> lea: But it's not a fallback. It's an assignment
    | <romain> q+
    | <fantasai> astearns: Maybe Lea can review all the places that param() is used and adjust the syntax?
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: E.g. param(var: value) ? It'd be a novel syntax, but not the worst idea...
    | <fantasai> ChrisL: You just asserted "I did it wrong, I should do it differently, let's back-organize everything"
    | <fantasai> ChrisL: That sounds like a general principle. Should ask TAG about it and get a general principle.
    | <ntim> foo.svg#:~:--foo=red&--bar=blue
    | <fantasai> ChrisL: Which means an explainer. Things done before, things done after, reasons.
    | <astearns> ack romain
    | <fantasai> romain: I'm concerned about ergonomics of (?) in the URL
    | <fantasai> romain: A lot of URLs are rewritten by tools
    | <fantasai> romain: e.g. rebased to where file is actually
    | <ntim> fantasai: no strong opinion, param() does seem a bit awkward though in the URL context
    | <fantasai> romain: Maybe have possibility to add these in a function? [uncertain]
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: Can't define a general function because this is part of URL syntax, and URLs are used everywhere. Can't define a new syntax in that space.
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: building on # is better
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: But if you are in CSS, you can use the url() function.
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: Or in a CSS property on the element
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: That passes it to any image it sees
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: There are easier ways to do it if in CSS, but if not in CSS, only way to do it is put it in the fragment
    | <TabAtkins> in CSS it's `url("foo.svg", param(--foo, red))`
    | <fantasai> Romain: Yes, need CSS syntax. URl-to-text-fragment syntax is awkward and hard to remember.
    | <romain> great!

- 2026-08-06 #3937 [css-overflow-4]
  RESOLVED: add this proposal to Overflow 5
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/3937#issuecomment-5203494437
  background: Hello,
  irc (60 lines):
    | <bramus> Explainer: https://github.com/sharonhsl/CSS-overflow-4-middle-truncation
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: some time ago I had a proposal about middle truncation in a strings
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: extending what we currently have with text-overflow
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: the original proposal was an out firebug use-cases, 2013
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: since then we've resolved on previous proposals for a specific syntax I had
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: but iv'e realized this proposal wasn't complete, missed some cases
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: I tried to come up with another
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: lately someone came up to me, sharon lam, and asked how she could help us get this pushed forward
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: you can see, the general use-case is to have the ellipsis in the middle of a string, useful for filenames, URLs, etc
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: useful for things where seeing the beginning and end of a string are both important
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: we had some goals and non-goals
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: this is targeting text-overflow, not line-clamping
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: current solutions use JS to mimic this, they're not great. can't handle edge cases, and change the actual value so they're not accessible and mess up copy/paste
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: browsers already implement middle-trunctation for <input type=file>
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: was discussing with florian, andreu, and emilio, checked with major browsers, they all show it
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: so the actual proposal
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: extend existing text-overflow, former resolution was to introduce new props, this is keeping it all inside existing text-overflow
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: extending in a way that allows middle truncation
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: what's new is the middle keyword in the grammar, and a length-percentage value that i'll explain in a moment
    | <bramus> q+
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: added start/end keywords for completeness, to explicitly indicate end-based ellipsis too
    | <astearns> q+
    | <romain> q-
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: [shows examples from the explainer]
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: text-overflwo: ellipsis middle;
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: `clip start ellipsis end`, etc
    | <florian> q+ to make a few remarks at the end of the presentation
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: to summarize how this works, we lean on what we already have
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: this is visual, doens't affect layout
    | <emilio> q+
    | <TabAtkins> `ellipsis 30%` or `ellipsis 3ch` counts back from the end
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: some impls don't handle scrolling very well right now, but we have a demo that shows it could work
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: [shows demos]
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: [shows off scrolling demo, the end of the text is fixed while beginning scrolls]
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: we think this works reasonably well. the overflow spec does specify some scrolling
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: are we looking to socialize this, or something more?
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: andreu fiddled a bit already, ahs a working chromium prototype
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: i'm searching for feedback. if we want to resolve on pursuing this approach, would be fantastic
    | <astearns> ack bramus
    | <ydaniv> q+
    | <TabAtkins> bramus: this is very well fleshed out, lot of thought clearly went into it
    | <TabAtkins> bramus: i find the % mapping a little confusing
    | <TabAtkins> bramus: start being 100% and end being 0%
    | <TabAtkins> florian: the usual assumption is that omitted % is 0%, but we count from the end here
    | <TabAtkins> bramus: I understand that, but 0%/100% usually map start/end
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: I originally had it like that, but thought if you had text-overflow:ellipsis it's at the end
    | <TabAtkins> bramus: can instead have that mean an omitted % is 100%
    | <fantasai> +1 to bramus
    | <TabAtkins> bramus: I think it's a little tricky to count characters back, but can use calc() like you demonstrate
    | <TabAtkins> yeah +1 too
    | <astearns> ack astearns
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: i'm a little bit concerned about value combinations that are invalid
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: but that is the way to do it if we're not yet sure how they'll work
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: if you make clip invalid you'll need to make empty string invalid too
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: because that's the same
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: what happens today if you use empty string?
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: we say it behaves the same as clip
    | <astearns> ack florian
    | <Zakim> florian, you wanted to make a few remarks at the end of the presentation
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: I excluded it because it doens't make sense, as a user, to have a clip in the middle, looks bad

    | <romain> q+

  RESOLVED: accentcolor and accentcolortext, UAs *should* return (and use) a fixed color as the initial (os-dependent) value, can expose real value in places where fingerprinting isn'ta  cocnern
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/10372#issuecomment-5202692386
  background: AccentColor/AccentColorText were created here: https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/7347
  related: #7347 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/7347
  irc (60 lines):
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: accentcolor and accentcolortext return the current color used from the OS level
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: some OSes let you change that color, like MacOS and windows
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: on Mac there are presets, on windows there's a color picker with free choice
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: so concern is exposing it to the document is a fingerprinting vector
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: a user might have a unique color
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: WebKit returns a static color, regardless of user's choice
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: I think that's what Chrome does too?
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: if the page is installed chrome can return the real color
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: I think that's from google's privacy team also thinking this is a fingerprinting vector
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: the current spec says UA has discretion to return the actual color or a hard coded value
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: are we okay with that? if it returns a hard coded color it's kinda useless
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: but if it returns the actual color, is there a way to prevent the page from inferring the actual color?
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: there was some discussion about making the color unobservable
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: for example by preventing the color from being used in canvas, or marking it as tainted
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: there are some other solutions we could explore
    | <TabAtkins> ntim: getComputedStyle() exposes it too
    | <emilio> q+
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: i'm fine with saying the UA can just lie
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: we've done that for all the other system colors
    | <astearns> ack emilio
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: the tainting stuff isn't conving to me
    | <lea> q+
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: it sounds really complicated to spec and implement to not leak it
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: for relatively little benefit
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: maybe a better approach is... I think we already resolved accentcolor reflects the accent-color property?
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: so via that resolution, accent-color is already not computed-value time, it's kept like currentcolor
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: I think it still resolves at computed value time
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: if it depends on accent-color the property, you need to delay it, right?
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: you could resolve it eagerly, I suppose, like em units
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: but I guess we didn't define the exact resolution mechanism
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: if we did that it's not a regular system color, it's just a magic keyword
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: I think it's kinda  a q about use-cases and impl complexity
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: don't think we talked about it much as an issue
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: currentcolor does need to reflect the current color even when inherited
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: but accentcolor isn't set for text and inherited, really
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: that's why currentcolor needs to compute to itself, but accentcolor doesn't really
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: so having it eagerly compute like em units is fine, I think, from the use-cases
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: and then it's much easier to manage for the browser
    | <kizu> q+
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: we refused to do this for canvas/canvastext
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: it doens't flip with color-scheme
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: if we're happy to add more suspended used-value colors we can do it for those. accentcolor isn't as important
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: I brought this up because if we keep it around as a keyword it's easier to say gCS() doesn't resolve the keyword
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: much easier than tainting
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: I personally wouldnt' go that route...
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: but if we're going to hide the accent color, keeping it computed to itself does simplify a lot
    | <astearns> ack lea
    | <TabAtkins> lea: i'm not super convinced that this is that big of a fingerprint vector, but not my specialty
    | <TabAtkins> lea: I think accentcolor that UAs actually use should be the same color authors have access to, otherwise you get mismatched UIs
    | <emilio> +1
    | <TabAtkins> lea: remind the group we have resolution to allow extracting components
    | <TabAtkins> lea: so without JS you can still read components
    | <TabAtkins> lea: even if we reversed that resolution you can exfil color components anyway via comparison
    | <astearns> ack kizu
    | <kizu> https://kizu.dev/svg-linked-parameters-workaround/#querrying-the-actual-color
    | <TabAtkins> kizu: I have an example
    | <TabAtkins> kizu: it works for anything besides currentcolor
    | <TabAtkins> kizu: once a color in a custom property you can read the components out and assign them to other custom props
    | <TabAtkins> kizu: you can't do it with a used value like currentcolor
    | <TabAtkins> kizu: component querying functions will have the same problem if we define it to not work for currentcolor, etc
- 2026-08-06 #12859 [css-overflow-4]
  RESOLVED: Make line-clamp consistent with overflow-y: clip. If there's a problem with that behavior we should fix it for both
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/12859#issuecomment-5206052656
  background: What should happen to content clamped by `line-clamp` in the accessibility tree?
  irc (60 lines):
    | <emilio> florian: this is one we resolved already but extra arguments were maid
    | <emilio> s/maid/made
    | <emilio> ... I think we resolved right but new arguments have come up
    | <emilio> ... line-clamp hides some lines, should they be visible in the a11y tree
    | <emilio> q+
    | <emilio> ... we decided not because they are like visibility: hidden
    | <emilio> ... james craig mentioned that one argument is that some users might not be completely blind and normally sighted user has larger zoom and could see way less content
    | <emilio> ... so it'd be a different experience for zoomed vs not
    | <emilio> ... so the content hidden should be in the a11y tree
    | <SebastianZ> q+
    | <emilio> florian: that doesn't make sense to me because a screen reader should read as stuff is being rendered
    | <emilio> ... media queries could also be affected by zoom
    | <ntim> what does overflow: hidden;text-overflow: ellipsis; do ?
    | <TabAtkins> +1 to treating sighted and screen readers the same here; the use-cases where you use line-clamp often also want to hide from screen readers
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: I think we shoudln't make the content taht's hidden inaccessible
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: so I'm with James but for different reasons
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: I don't think we've made content hidden by line-clamp unfocusable, unselectable, etc
    | <florian> q+
    | <andreubotella> q+
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: so even if it's line-clamped it'll still interact with find-in-page/etc. it's just clipped, we don't remove clipped things.
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: are you saying we should handle the same way as overflow?
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: yeah basically. this is also how text-overflow works
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: consider a text input with clipped text-overflow
    | <astearns> s/as overflow/as overflow-y:clip/
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: your screen reader still wants to read all of it
    | <TabAtkins> q+
    | <astearns> ack emilio
    | <astearns> ack SebastianZ
    | <emilio> SebastianZ: We should reach out to the visually impaired people to get feedback
    | <emilio> ... because it's hard to decide when they're not affecting you
    | <emilio> ... on the flip side I think it makes sense to either expose both or let the authors handle this
    | <emilio> ... the intention can be different
    | <emilio> q+
    | <emilio> ... they might want to show everything if there's enough space or clip the text but
    | <emilio> ... or maybe text may not be intended to be shown to users completely
    | <emilio> ... on the other hand if you have a short title is the other way around
    | <emilio> ... the optimal case you want to present it visually
    | <astearns> ack florian
    | <emilio> florian: I think emilio has a point
    | <emilio> ... having a disconnect on what's in the a11y tree and what's focusable would be weird
    | <emilio> ... overflow: hidden and so can still be scrolled-to
    | <emilio> ... when you end up having focusable content that's completely unreachable
    | <emilio> emilio: could be in the unscrollable area
    | <astearns> ack andreubotella
    | <emilio> ... that's the most common way of providing context to screen readers
    | <emilio> andreubotella: spec says the inflow and so after the clamp point those are invisible
    | <emilio> ... the definition of that says they can't be interacted with
    | <emilio> ... that's something that we haven't actually discussed, we added it in a spec PR without realizing the implications
    | <astearns> ack TabAtkins
    | <SebastianZ> q+
    | <astearns> ack emilio
    | <astearns> q+
    | <florian> q+
    | <emilio> emilio: I think we don't want line-clamp to affect focusability and so on like visibility has
    | <emilio> ... not many precedents for such a thing
    | <emilio> ... furthermore I don't think text-overflow hides text from a11y and I don't  think line-clamp should be different from that
    | <emilio> SebastianZ: I forgot to mention that if we only expose the clipped text to ATs then we still need some indication that the text is clipped
    | <kizu> q+

    | <astearns> ack SebastianZ
- 2026-08-06 #13786 [css-overflow-4]
  RESOLVED: use only the root inline strut when displacing a line due to block-ellipsis
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13786#issuecomment-5203554595
  background: When the `block-ellipsis` property (one of the `line-clamp` longhands) inserts an ellipsis, it can completely displace the contents of a line. When this happens, "the line box is considered to contain a [strut](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS21/visudet.html#strut), as defined in [CSS 2 § 10.8.1 Leading and half-leading](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS2/visudet.html#leading)." (The more recent non-CR definitio
  irc (20 lines):
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: we've talked about this a few times, if you have line-clamp:3 but ellipsis won't fit on third line, it'll displace the whole line
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: different from text-overflow behavior
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: spec says the displaced line is considered to have a strut
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: but this only talks about containing one strut
    | <florian> q+
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: but per spec there's one strut per inline box
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: so in this example, if we grow the font size of the nested span, does that grow the strut?
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: my understanding is when we were talking about the strut we were talking about root inline strut. but we didn't mention this explicitly or even acknowledge there might be multiple struts
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: so does it make sense to only use root inline, or consider struts from all inlines on the displaced line?
    | <TabAtkins> florian: yes it should be the root inline
    | <TabAtkins> florian: we need a strut at all because ellipsis doesn't contribute to line-height; we wanted to make sure it's impossible for it to change layout, and then we needed  a strut to keep the elite from collapsing
    | <TabAtkins> florian: we didn't need anything else
    | <TabAtkins> florian: I just didn't notice it even could be anything else, we should clarify it's the root inline strut only
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: so in this example the ellipsized line would be the same height as the first line?
    | <TabAtkins> florian: yes
    | <astearns> ack florian
    | <TabAtkins> TabAtkins: this makes sense to me, no need to have invisible things on the line have a visible effect
    | <TabAtkins> proposed: use only the root inline strut when displacing a line due to block-ellipsis
    | <florian> +1
    | <TabAtkins> RESOLVED: use only the root inline strut when displacing a line due to block-ellipsis

    | <bramus> … dont need to be arbitrary substitution functions either
    | <bramus> … functions that accept numbes already know to handle calc()_
    | <bramus> … calling them math functions might make sense
    | <bramus> emilio: easier to treat them as that
    | <bramus> … same way you resolve ems, you can now go to element (calculation context)
    | <bramus> … can also compute function to number itself
    | <bramus> … steps you need to take are not different from any other length unit and since you need to stash them in ??? implement once in calc tree versus inside+outside
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: this appears to be a general arg for any numeric functions
    | <kbabbitt> q+
    | <bramus> … any future fns that dont do calc but somehow return a number
    | <bramus> emilio: unless they can resolve at parse time
    | <bramus> … spec wise only meaningful diff is serialization
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: yes
    | <bramus> … for that, numbers, math fns, even if we resolve them away, we serialize with calc() wrapper
    | <bramus> emilio: no, a min is not a calc
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: but once you resolve away
    | <bramus> emilio: yes
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: it will retain fact that its a calc tree
    | <bramus> … for reason of auto clamping
    | <bramus> … so sibling-index() and cmoputed value might see calc(1)
    | <bramus> emilio: no, bc you can clamp at sibling index at computed value time
    | <bramus> astearns: maybe go to queueu?
    | <astearns> ack kbabbitt
    | <bramus> kbabbitt: if I follow correct, we talk about a class of tings that are valid in calc tree that ar enot mumbers or operators
    | <bramus> … wodering if there other things in that class, like color channel names
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: those are calc keywords
    | <bramus> … can use pi and e in there
    | <bramus> kbabbitt: gotcha
    | <emilio> document.body.style.zIndex // "calc(1)"
    | <emilio> getComputedStyle(document.body).zIndex // 1
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: other possibliy: channel extractor functions: fall under general argument
    | <astearns> ack fantasai
    | <bramus> fantasai: in the issue, there was a question about them being math functions or a general category of fns that get clamped/rounded but not math fns
    | <bramus> … and tab you have two resolutions you asked for?
    | <fantasai> https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13978#issuecomment-4606578252
    | <bramus> … one to say that all number returning fns also do range clamping
    | <bramus> … and to modify calc() serializaton for them so that they dont need the calc() wrapper
    | <bramus> … what would be distionction between doing that and making them any math function?
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: no author observable diff
    | <bramus> … if possible, would like to preserver math function keeping its meaning … carrying calc context with ith – OH, thats potentially important
    | <emilio> q+
    | <bramus> … it has a purpose for being of a defined class, and woul dlike to not use that for other things, but not make it observable
    | <bramus> emilio: can take of particular element as calculation context
    | <emilio> ack emilio
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: no, thats, what are the things that a number in that spot needs to know abut itself (e.g percentage resolving)
    | <bramus> … that is what a math fn brings you (in spec terms)
    | <astearns> ack fantasai
    | <bramus> fantasai: proposal to resolve on the two different behavioral differences
    | <bramus> … and create a term for all those functions that have that
- 2026-08-06 #14047 [css-overflow-4]
  RESOLVED: If the ellipsized line would fit back up next to the float, it moves up accordingly
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14047#issuecomment-5203745820
  background: When placed next to a float, a line whose smallest unwrappable content is to long to fit the remaining space is pushed down. However, `block-ellipsis` (as used by `line-clamp`) can make a line shorter when it removes content to make room for the ellipsis.
  irc (60 lines):
    | <TabAtkins> florian: this issue has subissues, but ther's a main topic and followups
    | <TabAtkins> florian: mainly about interaction for block-ellipsis and lineboxes on the side of floats and may or may not need clearance
    | <TabAtkins> florian: if you have a float that is reducing available width for line boxes
    | <TabAtkins> florian: and you have a linebox that doesn't fit, because it's shortest word is longer than that available width, the line is cleared below the float
    | <TabAtkins> florian: if that's the line where you place the block ellipsis, and the ellipsis is much shorter, now it *would have* fit next to the float
    | <TabAtkins> florian: do we keep it down? or move it back up given that it now fits?
    | <TabAtkins> q+
    | <TabAtkins> florian: I think the rest of the issues are probably easier, but let's discuss this one now
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: [demo]
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: with line-clamp:2, one behavior is the ellipsis fits here after the float, it gets positioned there, displacing the line
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: another is we put the ellipsis with he line, below the float
    | <TabAtkins> florian: the main one i'm talking about right now is just the "line would clear without ellipsis, can fit with ellipsis"
    | <TabAtkins> florian: there's also the other way around where the line does fit, but content+ellipsis doesn't fit, but would if you cleared
    | <TabAtkins> florian: so sticking with the first case for now
    | <TabAtkins> florian: shortening the line, do we bring it back up or not
    | <SebastianZ> q?
    | <astearns> ack TabAtkins
    | <emilio> TabAtkins: Given you can line-clamp based on a length
    | <emilio> ... your line-clamp instead of an int is a length
    | <emilio> ... the clear length causes that line to get ellipsized, would that matter?
    | <emilio> ... e.g. if line-clamp would be 2.5em
    | <emilio> andreubotella: in this example if it's only 2.5lh it'd be better impl wise to look at what's the last line fitting if we're not ellipsizing and work as if we had set liine-clamp to that number  of lines
    | <emilio> ... e.g for 2.5lh the result would be 2lh but it's fine for the content to be shorter than that
    | <emilio> ... if we agree that ellipsizing brings you back up
    | <SebastianZ> q+
    | <sgill> q+
    | <emilio> florian: if we agree that ellipsizing brings you back up, do we start pulling up more content?
    | <emilio> TabAtkins: that's cyclic, so no
    | <emilio> florian: so we agree on the second part
    | <emilio> ... but what about the first, would we bring that back up?
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: I think for this case, depending on the impl it might be hard to figure out where the line would be without ellipsizing
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: there are impls where you might lay out the elite without ellipsizing, then ellipsize it
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: but there are also ones where you know the second line will ellipsize so you'll do it as you lay it out originally
    | <astearns> ack SebastianZ
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: so I'd prefer to not depend on knowledge for where it "will go"; want to bring it up if it fits
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: clarifying: di dyou set height or line-height on this example?
    | <TabAtkins> florian: that's the next sub issue
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: I think moving the ellipsis back up sounds reasonable
    | <astearns> ack sgill
    | <TabAtkins> florian: I made that assumption when writing tests, just spec didn't support it so i'm asking
    | <TabAtkins> sgill: if you don't re-evaluate after ellipsizing, do you get a little more info on the context of that content?
    | <TabAtkins> sgill: would you know it was something extraordinarily long that didn't fit?
    | <TabAtkins> sgill: from a user persepctive
    | <TabAtkins> florian: you woudln't know more text... if you bring it up you know you miss something, if you keep it down you see a little more text and do know it's too long
    | <TabAtkins> florian: arguably it's some extra knowledge, but dont' think it's useful knowledge
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: queue is empty, do we resolve that an ellipsize line that fits next to the float moves back up?
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: note that block-ellpisis finds the previous linebreak point, so this only happens when you have a single unbreakable word
    | <TabAtkins> RESOLVED: If the ellipsized line would fit back up next to the float, it moves up accordingly
    | <TabAtkins> florian: the other part of the issue is a bit weirder, spec might already give an answer implciitly
    | <TabAtkins> florian: if your text does fit next to the float, and your text+ellipsis would fit after clearing without removing any additional text, should it clear?
    | <andreubotella> q+
    | <TabAtkins> florian: or should you remove text to keep the ellipsis on the same line?
    | <astearns> ack andreubotella
    | <TabAtkins> florian: my pref is the line is where it is, if you need to remove more text you remove it. don't search for another place you can move the line just to avoid removing text
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: if we do this, pushing the line down to add ellipsis, the result is taller than not clamping at all
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: so if we're clamping by a height, that's a problem
    | <TabAtkins> florian: and even by count, the goal is usually to keep it short, to having it get taller seems weird
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: I can't think of a reason why an author would want the more complex behavior
    | <TabAtkins> TabAtkins: checking, this isn't about if the ellipsis itself is too long to fit?
    | <TabAtkins> [discussion]

- 2026-08-06 #14067 [css-overflow-4]
  RESOLVED: line-clamp clips the same way as overflow-y: clip works
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14067#issuecomment-5205742203
  background: In #12665 and #13668, we resolved that inside a line-clamp container, floats are clipped to the block-end content edge of the container. But it's not clear how that should work when the container has a `border-radius`.
  related: #12665 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/12665, #13668 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13668
  irc (15 lines):
    | <emilio> florian: We said that floats longer than a thing line clamped we clip at the block end content edge
    | <emilio> ... we didn't specify whether it was shaped or not
    | <emilio> andreubotella: [demos it]
    | <emilio> florian: I think we should do un-shaped content edge
    | <emilio> andreubotella: if border-radius and that affects the clip that's no longer wanted
    | <emilio> q+
    | <astearns> ack emilio
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: the way i'd imagine this working is to basically do the same thing as overflow-y:clip
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: what does that do here?
    | <emilio> TabAtkins: border-radius doesn't clip to the radius, only the rectangle
    | <TabAtkins> (that is, overflowing content doesn't clip to the radius)
    | <emilio> emilio: I think we should do whatever overflow-y clip does
    | <emilio> fantasai: agreed it should be consistent, and if that is stupid we should fix that
    | <emilio> PROPOSED: line-clamp clips the same way as overflow-y: clip works
    | <emilio> RESOLVED: line-clamp clips the same way as overflow-y: clip works
- 2026-08-06 #14070 [css-color-5]
  RESOLVED: Don't omit alpha of 1 in relative color syntax
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14070#issuecomment-5202934726
  background: _Originally posted by @emilio in [#13992](https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13992#issuecomment-4744858827)_
  related: #13992 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13992
  irc (24 lines):
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: the spec says you can omit the alpha component in alpha(), which makes you look at the color you're relative to. but serialization also says you omit an alpha of 1
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: which Is wrong, when it's relative omitting does something different
    | <TabAtkins> q+
    | <TabAtkins> ntim: I think I'm with you due to variables...
    | <astearns> ack tuankiet65
    | <astearns> ack tuankiet
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: variables get substituted away, it's currentcolor that can do it
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: so my propoal is only omit `/1` in colors if not specified
    | <emilio> TabAtkins: when I read this earlier I thought it  was about the alpha() function but this is more general
    | <emilio> ... but yeah the alpha can't be omitted because in the general case they're not equivalent
    | <romain> +1
    | <emilio> ... so alpha is not omittable unless it was omitted in the first place
    | <emilio> PROPOSED: Don't omit alpha of 1 in relative color syntax
    | <romain> q+
    | <astearns> ack TabAtkins
    | <emilio> RESOLVED: Don't omit alpha of 1 in relative color syntax
    | <emilio> romain: did we also want to change alpha so that it's always required to specified something?
    | <TabAtkins> romain: Do we want to change alpha() so you can't omit the alpha component?
    | <ChrisL> I agree
    | <TabAtkins> [yesses all around]
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: yeah, alpha() is a special relative-only function that only changes the alpha, omitting alpha is silly
    | <fantasai> +1
    | <TabAtkins> proposed: alpha is required in alpha()
    | <TabAtkins> RESOLVED: alpha is required in alpha()

- 2026-08-06 #14133 [css-color-4]
  RESOLVED: Accept text in issue
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14133#issuecomment-5202305638
  background: https://drafts.csswg.org/css-color-4/#powerless-color-component
  irc (13 lines):
    | <TabAtkins> romain: when converting powerless components, powerless hue, to 0
    | <TabAtkins> romain: this can happen when saturation is 0, or very near
    | <TabAtkins> romain: when it's converted to another polar space with a significant chroma component, on the other end it's not powerless
    | <TabAtkins> romain: so proposal is to go ahead and force it to zero
    | <ChrisL> q+
    | <TabAtkins> romain: with a special carve out for black because it's not a single channel making it achromatic
    | <astearns> ack ChrisL
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: I agree, this gets rid of some floating point noise. we have proposed text in the issue
    | <TabAtkins> JohnJansen: this number doesn't show up in divisions?
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: no
    | <TabAtkins> TabAtkins: and if it does, 0 chroma happens naturally anyway
    | <TabAtkins> proposed: Accept text in issue
    | <TabAtkins> RESOLVED: Accept text in issue
- 2026-08-06 #14192 [css-color-4,css-ui-4]
  RESOLVED: AccentColor/AccentColorText on either 'color' or 'accent-color' looks at 'accent-color' on the parent.
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14192#issuecomment-5202764600
  background: https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/5900#issuecomment-2380321760 resolves that `AccentColor` should return the color set by `accent-color`, if one exists.
  related: #5900 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/5900
  irc (20 lines):
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: a while ago we resolved that accentcolor reflects the 'accent-color' prop
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: if it's set to a value other than 'auto'
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: I was implementing, but ran into this issue
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: 1) possibly to have a cycle where accent-color:currentcolor and color:accentcolor
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: proposed is when color is accentcolor it's the accentcolor of the parent
    | <ntim> PROPOSED: accent-color: AccentColor resolves to the accent-color of the parent
    | <TabAtkins> TabAtkins: how do we do this with lh and em?
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: there's an order, font-size:1lh looks at parent's line-height too
    | <tuankiet65> q+
    | <TabAtkins> TabAtkins: okay jsu t making sure it's consistent
    | <TabAtkins> proposed: 'color' prop set to accentcolor/accentcolortext looks at parent's values
    | <astearns> ack tuankiet65
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: this matches what I already implemented so it's good
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: AccentColor/AccentColorText on either 'color' or 'accent-color' looks at 'accent-color' on the parent.
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: there's another discussion about current background, should we generalize the resolution?
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: prefer to keep it specific for now, can put it in the same bucket when it comes up
    | <ChrisL> +1
    | <emilio> +1
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: AccentColor/AccentColorText on either 'color' or 'accent-color' looks at 'accent-color' on the parent.
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: so next is the timing of when accentcolor is resolved
- 2026-08-06 #14192 [css-color-4,css-ui-4]
  RESOLVED: 'AccentColor' and 'AccentColorText' resolve against the element's own 'accent-color' in 'color' (and everywhere else other than 'accent-color' itself)
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14192#issuecomment-5202877510
  background: https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/5900#issuecomment-2380321760 resolves that `AccentColor` should return the color set by `accent-color`, if one exists.
  related: #5900 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/5900
  irc (10 lines):
    | <TabAtkins> PROPOSED: using accentcolor or accentcolortext in 'accent-color' looks at parent; using elsewhere (including 'color') is fine and resolves as normal
    | <tuankiet65> q+
    | <astearns> (changing previous resolution based on discussion in https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14193)
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: 'AccentColor' and 'AccentColorText' resolve against the element's own 'accent-color' in 'color' (and everywhere else other than 'accent-color' itself)
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: Given the timing resolution we just had, there's no cycle.
    | <TabAtkins> [reviewed the resolution again]
    | <fantasai> TabAtkins: if we resolved at the same time (both computed or both used) would be a problem
    | <ntim> +1
    | <kbabbitt> So if `accent-color: currentcolor` and `color: accentcolor` the resolution goes: color looks at accent-color, which is currentcolor, which looks at parent color
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: 'AccentColor' and 'AccentColorText' resolve against the element's own 'accent-color' in 'color' (and everywhere else other than 'accent-color' itself)
- 2026-08-06 #14193 [css-color-4,css-ui-4]
  RESOLVED: accentcolor/accentcolortext resolve at computed value time
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14193#issuecomment-5202841799
  background: https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/5900#issuecomment-2380321760 resolves that `AccentColor` should return the color set by `accent-color`, if one exists.
  related: #5900 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/5900
  irc (25 lines):
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: in my impl it resolves accentcolor during computed value
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: that has some complication... some things have to be done in order
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: accent-color first, then color, then all other color properties
    | <TabAtkins> tuankiet65: which is a bit annoyhing
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: that's how color-scheme works too,
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: so ordering is color-scheme, accent-color, color, others
    | <TabAtkins> TabAtkins: and same as the ordering needed for font-size, line-height
    | <fantasai> fantasai: but that is the opposite of the cycle break we just resolved on?
    | <tuankiet65> Sorry, I need to check what's the order that I implemented
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: what's the more useful use-case?
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: color:accentcolor is more important than accent-color:currentcolor
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: yes, so therefore we need to swap the previous resolution
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: yes, color:accentcolor should bea ble to look at the actual accent-color of the element
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: so accent-color:currentcolor looks at the parent
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: no it's fine. it computes to itself
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: accent-color:currentcolor; color:accentcolor;
    | <TabAtkins> emilio: in that case, at computed time, accent-color remains currentcolor keyword. color look at accent-color, so effectively you're doing color:currentcolor, so you'll get the color of your parent
    | <TabAtkins> fantasai: so we need to go back and change the resolution a bit
    | <TabAtkins> PROPOSED: accentcolor/accentcolortext resolve at computed value time
    | <fantasai> +1
    | <tuankiet65> +1
    | <emilio> +1
    | <ChrisL> +1
    | <ntim> PROPOSED: color: AccentColor; resolves to the current accent-color
    | <TabAtkins> RESOLVED: accentcolor/accentcolortext resolve at computed value time
- 2026-08-06 #14207 [css-color-4]
  RESOLVED: Add ok2r as distance measure
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14207#issuecomment-5202279997
  background: Delta E is a class of algorithms to measure the actual perceived difference between two colors.
  related: #6642 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/6642
  irc (60 lines):
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: Do people know what deltaE is?
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: okay, it's a measure of how different two colors are
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: if they're the same it's 0, then it gets larger
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: And you want it to correspond well to human vision
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: different measures do this better or worse
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: so the industry generally uses deltaE2000, the fourth iteration of some ISO standard
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: it's in the appendix for how to do it in JS. it's slow but it gets results
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: we also have deltaEOK, which is just geom distance between the points in oklab, it's all right but very fast
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: oklab creator said a and b axises are too short
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: he did some experiments, and found the correction ratios were 2-ish
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: I tested this
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: in the issue you'll see a scatter plot, I generated a synthetic scatterplot
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: for each point a spread of values and measured their distance in dE2000 and dEOK and dEOK2, the scaled one
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: you'll see the residual line looks better for the new one
    | <TabAtkins> (deOK2 matches dE2000 better)
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: so I was ready to suggest switchin, then a new commenter
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: dEOK assumes the color si by itself, basically dark mode. but it's usually against white, you need a different curve
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: theres' a variant okLRAB, so dEOK2LR, the scatterplot is even better
    | <florian> q+
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: so, do we go for the good enough ok2, or do we add the even better one
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: and if we do the better deltaE, do we add it just as that, or as a color space too?
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: can you tell me what that Algo does in the spec?
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: it's used for any color measurement stuff, like the gamut mapping
    | <TabAtkins> kbabbitt: will this impact contrast-color()?
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: no, it's not affecting the lightness plane
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: your first analysis didn't consider perceiving the color on a light background. is it only on a white background or is it also good on darker?
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: that's the thing. oklab is better on darker bgs
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: let me pull up a link...
    | <ChrisL> https://github.com/svgeesus/deltaE-OK2/tree/main
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: this is the repo where I did all of this
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: you can see there if you scroll a bit, there are four plots
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: deOK vs 2000, not great. ok2 is definitely better
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: and 2000 is in the spec?
    | <romain> q+
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: it's in the spec as the "best" but we don't recommend it because it's expensive. currently deOK is what we recommend
    | <florian> q?
    | <ChrisL> https://apps.colorjs.io/lr-vs-l/?color1=%2300000F&color2=black
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: and to help people understand, I made another app
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: it lets you compare interpolation in oklab vs oklrab
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: and lets you swap bg from black to white
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: but that's more for "should we add this as another color space"
    | <TabAtkins> florian: some qs. how important is it that we measure the same way others do? how problematic/un is it to have a moving target?
    | <TabAtkins> florian: do we break compat over time?
    | <TabAtkins> florian: and another q, is there a worldview where it could make sense to have the author supplying their own function?
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: measuring the same way as others isn't quite the way to put it. we'r measuring how different two colors appear. we have the "good" Algo that's slow and an okay one in the spec we use. gamut mapping needs good and fast.
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: the impact on gamut mapping will be slight, I think. not major, but slightly better.
    | <TabAtkins> florian: so if we don't match Photoshop or something it wont' really matter?
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: not much, no. small details. at some point, invisible details
    | <TabAtkins> florian: so if in a few years we come up with something slightly better it's okay to switch?
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: right
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: and note ATM there are 3 algos in the spec and we let impls choose, so there's already possible variation.k they're all extremely better than channel clipping.
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: this would replace one of the algos?
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: I'd probably leave them in, just recommend the new ok2 algo
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: i'd change the gamut mappers to use ok2
    | <astearns> ack florian
    | <TabAtkins> ChrisL: 2000 is in the spec for understanding, but we don't actually use it, same thing
    | <astearns> ack romain
    | <TabAtkins> romain: I agree
    | <TabAtkins> romain: clarify, most gamut mapping algos, particular the favored ones, dont' use this at all
    | <TabAtkins> romain: only the original do
- 2026-08-06 #14213 [css-values-5]
  RESOLVED: ident() is an arbitrary substitution function
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14213#issuecomment-5205278180
  background: Apparently it makes implementing simpler. @anttijk or @emilio can elaborate
  irc (60 lines):
    | <bramus> ntim: antti and emilio brought up `ident()` prolly needing to be a arbitrary substitution function as its easier to implement
    | <bramus> … otherwise need to preserver until value time
    | <bramus> … right now just a substitution function one
    | <bramus> … and should be “arbitrary substitution function”
    | <bramus> … spec does not specify replacement steps
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: correct, because it currently is not a “substitution function”
    | <emilio> q+
    | <TabAtkins> q_
    | <TabAtkins> q+
    | <bramus> ntim: we thought it was just a normal function when implementing, but antti and emilio said it should be “arbitrary substitution function”
    | <astearns> ack emilio
    | <bramus> emilio: main arg is that if point of ident() to be used in arbitrary substitution functions and not in arbritrary things that can take idents
    | <bramus> … its a lot easier to deal with it … same reasoning for attr() … it was made arbitrary substitution function instead of typed as it goes from swiping through codebase vs dealing with it in variable scope and after that its gone
    | <bramus> … implemtnation wise easier to do arbitrary substitution function
    | <keithamus> q?
    | <bramus> … otherwise you need to change the representation of eveyr identifier in CSS to support this fn
    | <bramus> … much more complicated
    | <bramus> … while only benefit is that you get some sort of type checking
    | <bramus> … ident in width vs being arbitrary substitution function that fails and becomes IACVT
    | <bramus> … impl wise the answer is obvious
    | <bramus> … or do we want to complicate things?
    | <keithamus> q+
    | <bramus> ntim: other arg by antti for arbitrary substitution function is that var(ident) wouldnt work
    | <ntim> var(ident(--))
    | <ntim> var(ident(...))
    | <TabAtkins> var(ident(...)) would work
    | <bramus> ntim: curious if there are we implications for what devs can do if we change
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: there is
    | <astearns> ack TabAtkins
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: so, first, the assertion about why attr() is arbitrary substitution function is not quite right. it is because it can resolve to any type and cant know that until you look at the value
    | <emilio> q+
    | <bramus> … so just like var() in that case
    | <bramus> … ident() doe snot have that problem
    | <bramus> … all the args are things you can see that will always be syntactically valid (unless you put a var() in it, but thats for teverything)
    | <bramus> … from that perspected, ident() does not need to be arbitrary substitution function, its an identifier
    | <bramus> … however, there are some things you cant do if this is a arbitrary substitution function
    | <bramus> … such as the very first example in the spec: using sibling-index()
    | <bramus> emilio: no
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: oh, that would be fine
    | <bramus> emilio: that is a good arg for making sibling-index() an arbitrary substitution function
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: its an int
    | <bramus> emilio: but attr() with no arg is a string
    | <bramus> … dont typecheck
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: (replies)
    | <RRSAgent> I have made the request to generate https://www.w3.org/2026/08/06-css-minutes.html fantasai
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: so if there are any things that we might want to put in ident(0 would not resolve by computed value time
    | <bramus> … that would  be aproblem as you have to keep ident() around
    | <bramus> … broader problem, as more places need to know what the ident is by computed value time
    | <bramus> … having it arbitrary substitution function would force our hand there, arbitrary substitution function always disappear by computed value time
    | <bramus> … so we would make it invalid if the args dont fully resolve at that time
    | <bramus> … thats argument for
    | <bramus> … and more generally, its complicated to support calc() everywhere, as all that take it need to know how to deal with it
    | <bramus> … not worth doing that a second time for all things that take an identifier
    | <bramus> … ok with it being a arbitrary substitution function but does not actually need to be
    | <astearns> q?
    | <bramus> … its a convenience for us and ok to do
    | <astearns> ack keithamus
    | <bramus> keithamus: is the fallout of that that you can do `color: ident()`
    | <bramus> TabAtkins: yes, would be possible either way
    | <bramus> … rule would be that ident() is arbitrary substitution function and needs to parse to one ident and otherwise IACVT
- 2026-08-06 #14239 [css-overflow-4]
  RESOLVED: close no change, update tests
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14239#issuecomment-5203810533
  background: The spec for the `block-ellipsis` property (which is part of `line-clamp`) says the ellipsis isn't counted for `min/max-content`:
  irc (25 lines):
    | <TabAtkins> florian: spec says block-ellipsis has no effect on intrinsic sizes. min/max-content are calculated as if it wasn't there.
    | <TabAtkins> florian: do we mean this? I think yes
    | <TabAtkins> florian: it would be weird if changing the # of lines as you clamp changed the intrinsic size
    | <TabAtkins> florian: could potentially introduce circularities, like in Grid
    | <TabAtkins> florian: if removing lines due to available height changes your intrinsic height which changes layout....
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: seems fine in that example
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: example, first word is long + more words, line-clamp:1, you might have the width is sized to content without ellipsis, but after adding ellipsis you dont' have space so word is kicked out, now you have just an ellipsis in an empty space. a little weird but tprobably ok
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: there are some wpt tests abo9ut baselines that are affected by this
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: but they're not testing this interation on purpose
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: just noticed it
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: ellipsis es changing the baseline of a line because it's displacing the inlines
    | <TabAtkins> florian: I think this does result  sometimes in reserving more space than you visually need, but doing otherwise introduces complications I dont' think we need
    | <SebastianZ> q+
    | <TabAtkins> andreubotella: i think it's fine, just some cases where you get only an ellipsis as a result
    | <TabAtkins> florian: so the resolution i'm looking for is no change
    | <astearns> ack SebastianZ
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: looking at current spec, it says this already?
    | <TabAtkins> florian: yes, it says block-ellipsis has no effect on intrinsic sizes.
    | <TabAtkins> florian: you never get shorter intrinsically from block-ellipsis
    | <TabAtkins> SebastianZ: looking at a demo, I'd expect ellipsizing not removing a long word when it doesn't fit
    | <TabAtkins> [demo is not on screen, scribe is confused]
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: so proposed resolution is close no change, update tests
    | <TabAtkins> florian: yes
    | <TabAtkins> astearns: objections?
    | <TabAtkins> RESOLVED: close no change, update tests
