- 2026-08-19 #13774 [css-text-4]
  RESOLVED: we allow 'normal replace' as a value for text-autospace
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13774#issuecomment-5345036821
  background: https://www.w3.org/TR/css-text-4/#propdef-text-autospace
  irc (41 lines):
    | <JoshT> florian: text-autospace fundamentally have values that are punctuation, idiograph, etc.
    | <fantasai> <autospace> = no-autospace | [ ideograph-alpha || ideograph-numeric || punctuation ] || [ insert | replace ]
    | <JoshT> ... it has no autospace, ideograph apha, alphaneumetic and punctionation
    | <JoshT> ... and it has two modes. default is insert where it adds the space if it's not there
    | <JoshT> ... and then replace. if there is a space already but the wrong size, it will put the right size
    | <JoshT> ... so if you have a space glyth between a kanji, we remove that and add the right space
    | <JoshT> ... now you can only choose to insert and replace with the keywords here. you can't do that with the normal keyword.
    | <JoshT> ... idioalpha and idio-alphaneumeric together are the same as normal
    | <JoshT> ... and the i18n people want to use the replace keyword together with normal without the expanded version
    | <JoshT> ... depends on what magic we want normal to do
    | <JoshT> ... not having options next to it will keep our hands free for the future
    | <JoshT> ... but this might be a nice syntax convinience
    | <JoshT> ... but normal is the only thing implemented so far. so you can't opt into replace with the longhand versions because it's not there yet
    | <vitorroriz> WebKit also implements auto, but I think no browser implements replace (?)
    | <JoshT> ... if the problem is that it's not implemented, it should be implemented. another thing doesn't help
    | <vitorroriz> sorry, I mean normal*
    | <JoshT> ... so do we want to require people to use the expanded version in case we want normal to be more magic in the future
    | <JoshT> ... or maybe we want auto for magic?
    | <JoshT> ... I'm not convinced for the implementation argument, but can go either way otherwise
    | <JoshT> astearns: one thing about the impl issue: when a browser impl everything, is the shorthand still useful?
    | <JoshT> florian: behaviour wise it's the same but less typing
    | <jfkthame> q+
    | <vitorroriz> q+
    | <JoshT> ... but if we want normal to evolve...
    | <astearns> ack jfkthame
    | <JoshT> jfkthame: the ideograph-alpha and -alphaneumeric are implemented in gecko, but not replace
    | <JoshT> ... depends on whether insert or replace is the one people will most commonly want
    | <JoshT> ... normal now means ig-alpha
    | <JoshT> ... and it's insert
    | <JoshT> ... so it would be a change to what it currently means
    | <JoshT> florian: oh they're not asking for the normal behaviour to be switched to replace. they want to write 'normal replace'
    | <JoshT> jfkthame: so normal is simply a shorthand for the two other values? I guess I don't have a strong opion either way. I wonder how commonly people will want the replace vlaue?
    | <astearns> ack vitorroriz
    | <JoshT> vitorroriz: when you say concern is that normal could evolve somehow where we don't want to pair it with replace, if we want to have this magic, we shouldn't change it... can you give an example how normal could evolve?
    | <JoshT> florian: this is theoretical. this assumes that normal or its expansion will have two modes. if we want it to change to different modes, if the thing that normal expands to isn't a thing with two modes, etc, then having a mode switch would be weird
    | <JoshT> ... but nothing like that has been proposed
    | <JoshT> astearns: some language where if normal processing is not the same as ig-alpha, for that language normal would have some processing not part of the spec... but I don't have an example of where that would happen
    | <JoshT> ... given the request from the language experts and no clear way how we would want to evolve normal, I am inclined to support this syntax
    | <JoshT> florian: I don't think it's too needed but I don't think it's a problem
    | <JoshT> PROPOSED: we allow 'normal replace' as a value for text-autospace
    | <JoshT> RESOLVED: we allow 'normal replace' as a value for text-autospace
- 2026-08-19 #13789 [css-text-4]
  RESOLVED: use the syntax linked in IRC with the addition of the auto keyword
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13789#issuecomment-5344892684
  background: The current definition of text-spacing-trim is hard for content authors to get their head around, because each value affects several aspects of text-spacing at a time. There may also be combinations that authors want to apply that can't be done. The current definition is:
  irc (54 lines):
    | <JoshT> florian: text-spacing-trim is dealing with dropping the empty half of asian punc
    | <JoshT> ... whether at start of line or end, if you have opening or closing parenths
    | <JoshT> ... we had defined a set of values which attempt to get the useful behaviours rather than all possibilities
    | <JoshT> ... so trimming on start, end, both, etc... we have limited it to things we think people will actually us
    | <JoshT> s/us/use
    | <JoshT> ... the set of names is super non-obvious
    | <JoshT> ... the i18n group said our values are weird, people need to understand the system to even decide what to do
    | <JoshT> ... so that would make it simpler to spec, but would it make it easier for authors to think through? it doesn't seem great to let authors set values that no one will use
    | <JoshT> ... any additions or corrections?
    | <JoshT> astearns: three options? stick to what we have, remove and replace with the individual options, or do both
    | <JoshT> florian: yes, correct. I don't think there are naming clashes for 'both'
    | <JoshT> astearns: we should take the feedback that the terms we chose are not the best names, if the i18n folks struggled to understand them
    | <JoshT> florian: for me the key question: it doesn't seem like in the right order for priority of constituencies, but are people going to implement these with only some variants anyway? will having all the variants make it slower?
    | <vitorroriz> I like the idea of exposing the directives but I believe the current proposed axes on the issue can't express the current values on the spec, like trim-all
    | <vitorroriz> q+
    | <JoshT> ... if it makes the whole thing more complicated and less performant, I wouldn't want to do it
    | <emilio> q+
    | <astearns> ack vitorroriz
    | <JoshT> vitorroriz: on webkit, we haven't implemented trim
    | <JoshT> ... I like the proposed idea, because I myself can't remember what all the combos mean
    | <JoshT> ... I don't believe it would be more complicated. maybe a bit easier
    | <JoshT> ... but for the proposed accents, I don't think they can express all of the possible options like trim-all
    | <astearns> ack fantasai
    | <JoshT> ... I would either prefer exposing all of the options or go for the hybrid option
    | <JoshT> fantasai: so I think I came up with the same syntax ?? that would make sense, allow the existing set and broken out set
    | <JoshT> ... I don't have strong opinion either way, but I think we should insist on single keyword approach and provide the split out approach for everyone
    | <fantasai> s/??/as Murakami-san/
    | <JoshT> emilio: without impl experience, I don't know if it will be more complicated or not to expose all of the knobs
    | <JoshT> ... wouldn't object to breaking things up
    | <JoshT> ... probably would be nice to wait until there's some existing implementations to see web compat
    | <JoshT> astearns: so impl feedback is that this is plausible
    | <JoshT> ... do we want to go ahead with the individual bits and do the single keywords later?
    | <astearns> ack emilio
    | <JoshT> florian: I think either go with everything or keep the combo values
    | <JoshT> ... I have a preference for limiting the ???
    | <JoshT> ... but I think we're leaning the other way towards the syntax proposed by fantasai and Murakami-san
    | <JoshT> fantasai: people are shipping the single keyword version
    | <JoshT> astearns: we could remove it if usage is low
    | <JoshT> fantasai: in some cases, the single keyword option will give you the default on the other side
    | <JoshT> ... trim-both is a shorthand for setting all three settings
    | <JoshT> ... only one of the three keywords can't be represented by the breakdown
    | <JoshT> astearns: would you be OK punting on that for now or fix this first?
    | <JoshT> vitorroriz: I thought trim-all couldn't be represented by the proposal?
    | <JoshT> fantasai: no that's right. we would need trim-adjacent or maybe trim-all slides into that
    | <JoshT> florian: I don't expect someone will want trim-all in the middle and space-end, so your syntax makes sense to me
    | <JoshT> astearns: I am confused what the proposed syntax actually is
    | <JoshT> florian: the expended axes are the same, but fantasai 's proposal is more exhaustive
    | <JoshT> fantasai: Murakami-san has trim-auto, which was the previous name of trim-both
    | <astearns> https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13789#issuecomment-5177555212
    | <JoshT> astearns: we are going to resolve on the syntax in fantasai 's comment
    | <JoshT> florian: the only thing missing is auto, but that should just be added
    | <JoshT> PROPOSED: use the syntax linked in IRC with the addition of the auto keyword
    | <JoshT> astearns: any objections?
    | <JoshT> RESOLVED: use the syntax linked in IRC with the addition of the auto keyword
- 2026-08-19 #14003 [css-content-3]
  RESOLVED: Allow predefined symbolic counter keywords directly in 'content'
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14003#issuecomment-5344624949
  background: See sibling issue https://github.com/whatwg/html/issues/12516. HTML defines base-select as using `content: counter(fake-counter-name, disclosure-open)` seemingly to get around wanting `disclosure-open` but not really wanting to use a counter.
  irc (26 lines):
    | <ydaniv> keithamus: the content counter uses a dummy counter name, because syntax requires it.
    | <ydaniv> ... it's really weird, and it's there cause of legacy
    | <fantasai> content: disclosure-open
    | <masonf> Just do s/fake/specified and it'll be less weird
    | <lea> q+
    | <astearns> ack lea
    | <keithamus> `content: counter(fake-counter-name, disclosure-open)` -> `content: disclosure-open`
    | <ydaniv> lea: +1 on being able to define easilly, not sure about list-style type, the keyword would look weird
    | <fantasai> https://drafts.csswg.org/css-counter-styles/#simple-symbolic
    | <ydaniv> TabAtkins: the proposal is the current predfined static names, disc/circle/etc.
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: yes only predefined ones
    | <ydaniv> lea: ok seems fine then
    | <emilio> +1 to this
    | <masonf> +1 also
    | <astearns> ack fantasai
    | <Zakim> fantasai, you wanted to reply
    | <lwarlow> +1 ship it
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Allow predefined symbolic counter keywords directly in 'content'
    | <ydaniv> astearns: so we allow these predefined keywords, but will still work with author content
    | <lwarlow> +1
    | <masonf> +1
    | <ydaniv> keithamus: yes
    | <keithamus> +1
    | <kizu> +1
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Allow predefined symbolic counter keywords directly in 'content'
    | <ydaniv> astearns: objections?
- 2026-08-19 #14317 [css-forms-1]
  RESOLVED: Add new list-style keywords for picker icons. The opposite states must be rotated versions of the same glyph around an appropriate center point. Use these in 'content' for ::picker-icon.` (Names TBD.)
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14317#issuecomment-5344573035
  background: The UA stylesheet for `<select>` currently uses `disclosure-open` for the `::picker-icon`. However this kind of large filled triangle tends to be too bold and pointy in comparison to the adjacent text, which has led many design systems, including the demo styles from last week's `<select>` styling discussion, to adopt a chevron design.
  related: #14003 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14003
  irc (57 lines):
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: one of the things we need to change for select styling is change the disclosure icon to another chevron
    | <ydaniv> ... we did something hacky and we should not do that
    | <ydaniv> ... so question is how we do that
    | <ydaniv> ... we have picker icons, etc.
    | <TabAtkins> +1 to the idea obviously ^_^
    | <ydaniv> ... second question is what keyword we want?
    | <florian> q+
    | <ydaniv> astearns: these picker open/closed would have the magic putting the glyph in right position?
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: yes
    | <keithamus> We already have internal magic for auto appearance: https://searchfox.org/firefox-main/source/widget/Theme.cpp#715-767
    | <ydaniv> TabAtkins: we have an example unicode glyph, and you can make it how you wish
    | <fantasai> https://drafts.csswg.org/css-counter-styles/#simple-symbolic
    | <masonf> q+
    | <ydaniv> astearns: is that going to be an issue if 2 different browsers choose different renderings? Does it need to be consistent?
    | <lwarlow> q+
    | <ydaniv> TabAtkins: as long as roughly same shape/size should be fine
    | <JakeA> q+
    | <ydaniv> ... historically everyone chose similar icon
    | <ydaniv> ... it's ok to have some degree of variations
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: for example different fonts
    | <astearns> ack florian
    | <ydaniv> florian: is the glyph itself magically set?
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: yes
    | <astearns> ack masonf
    | <fantasai> s/set/writing-mode aware/
    | <keithamus> q+
    | <ydaniv> masonf: the disclosure icon is rendering same roughly on browsers, so how do we handle bugs on some rendering ugly?
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: for now you have some room for interpretation
    | <astearns> acK lwarlow
    | <ydaniv> lwarlow: for me it's mostly about looking roughly the same, size mostly the same, and rendering consitently
    | <ydaniv> ... so if some browser is a bit different it's not an issue, so need to specify too strict
    | <ydaniv> ... make sure height doesn't change
    | <ydaniv> ... it's possible to be different on different OS's
    | <astearns> ack JakeA
    | <ydaniv> JakeA: other ppl like to spin the icon on open/close, so might be worth allowing animating the same icon on open/close
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: makes sense
    | <astearns> ack keithamus
    | <ydaniv> keithamus: disagree with lwarlow, problem that rendering glyph is not always consistent, might cause visual issues
    | <ydaniv> ... the disclosure triangle ppl will change cause it's ugly, but still ppl will change for consistency
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: we already icons that differ based on selected font, like checkmark icon
    | <lwarlow> Design systems should use their own iconography anyway. Like if they care that much really.
    | <ydaniv> keithamus: I see what mean, so we're not improving, so we're making worse?
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: don't think so, more allowing ppl who want to change specifically and allowing them
    | <ydaniv> TabAtkins: if we want in the future we can always swap it
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: with JakeA's caveat, it makes sense
    | <fantasai> PROPOSAL: Add new list-style keywords for picker icons. The opposite states must be rotated versions of the same glyph around an appropriate center point.
    | <ydaniv> keithamus: should the icon have a fixed layout?
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: we'll have it like other icon types
    | <ydaniv> ... and we have to figure out the name
    | <fantasai> PROPOSAL: Add new list-style keywords for picker icons. The opposite states must be rotated versions of the same glyph around an appropriate center point. Use these in 'content' for ::picker-icon.
    | <lwarlow> +1
    | <davidjmarland> +1
    | <masonf> +1
    | <ydaniv> astearns: objections to proposal?
    | <ydaniv> lwarlow: the list-style keywords might not work here?
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: that's the next issue
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Add new list-style keywords for picker icons. The opposite states must be rotated versions of the same glyph around an appropriate center point. Use these in 'content' for ::picker-icon.
- 2026-08-19 #14323 [css-forms-1]
  RESOLVED: Add ::field-text wrapping the selected content representation when no button element provided; investigate compat to potentially apply also when button is provided, to give a more consistent API.
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14323#issuecomment-5344363700
  background: This is a follow-up from https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14255. The `<select>` element has two possible markup patterns. "Customizable select" adds an extra `button` and optional `selectedcontent` element to represent the duplicated `option` content and allow it to be styled independently. But traditional markup doesn't have these elements, it just contains `option`s.
  related: #13516 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/13516, #14255 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14255
  irc (60 lines):
    | <ydaniv> ScribeNick: ydaniv
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: issue is about the select menu button text
    | <fantasai> [ selectedcontent ::picker-icon ]
    | <ydaniv> ... the selected contents and picker-icon
    | <ydaniv> ... problem is the contents is only exists if you use the extended syntax
    | <ydaniv> ... it's new and bunch of extra typings
    | <ydaniv> ... it was designed to do more interesting things
    | <ydaniv> ... but for basic stuff to just show up...
    | <ydaniv> ... if you have a selected option text is longer than the select, since it's a flexbox, it will push the picker icon out of the button and it'll overflow
    | <kizu> q+
    | <ydaniv> ... so you have a select button with just overflow text and no icon
    | <ydaniv> ... in other form controls we've added field text pseudo element which let's us select the value in it
    | <emilio> q+
    | <lwarlow> q+
    | <keithamus> q+
    | <ydaniv> ... so proposal is to reuse that same pseudo element to select the selected content of that form control that allows us to style it, so we can override flexbos properties
    | <lwarlow> Do you have a demo of this case where it pushes the picker-icon out of the box? I don't doubt it, it would be useful to see it in action though
    | <astearns> ack kizu
    | <kizu> https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/2406
    | <fantasai> https://drafts.csswg.org/css-forms-1/#selectordef-field-text
    | <ydaniv> kizu: I think it's a good idea, only thing is there is also a proposal for generic pseudo contents element
    | <ydaniv> ... so we could use this ::contents and it will work
    | <ydaniv> ... not sure we want another specific element
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: problem is it will also overflow the picker icon
    | <ydaniv> kizu: it was defined before the wrap auto, wonder if we could design to position it elsewhere
    | <astearns> ack fantasai
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: we already have field-text for this purpose to style the input
    | <astearns> ack emilio
    | <ydaniv> emilio: I think it makes sense, question is, how do you deal with the [missed]
    | <astearns> s/[missed
    | <ydaniv> ... I don't think we want to match that with appearance
    | <astearns> s/[missed]/auto appearance/
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: we're not exposing the icon in appearance auto
    | <ydaniv> emilio: I guess I'll file an issue about it
    | <astearns> ack lwarlow
    | <emilio> ack emilio
    | <ydaniv> lwarlow: regarding that, there's an issue on that, I think makes sense not to expose in appearance:auto
    | <ydaniv> ... would be good to have a basic option in the browser to expose this
    | <ydaniv> ... not all of the text is well defined, don't know if all is exposed
    | <ydaniv> ... so makes sense renaming it
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: that's the next issue
    | <ydaniv> astearns: lwarlow could you link the issue?
    | <ydaniv> ... so it's better to specify properly
    | <astearns> ack keithamus
    | <ydaniv> lwarlow: yes
    | <JakeA> q+
    | <ydaniv> keithamus: so is it selecting the selected content?
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: yes
    | <lwarlow> q+
    | <ydaniv> keithamus: so we want to select the first child of the button?
    | <masonf> q+
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: the problem is you don't have a first-child option
    | <davidjmarland> lwarlow: https://codepen.io/editor/djmarland/pen/019f1daf-4d52-7ffd-8edd-fba1ac5b0d4a
    | <lwarlow> https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/12365
    | <ydaniv> ... it's just option, option, option ...
    | <lwarlow> Here's the issue about exposing outside of base appearance or not
    | <ydaniv> keithamus: so we're selecting the generated label?
    | <ydaniv> fantasai: yeah
    | <ydaniv> keithamus: so we have multi select content
    | <lwarlow> No
- 2026-08-20 #14249 [css-forms-1]
  RESOLVED: Add box-sizing to internal form control parts as described in the issue.
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14249#issuecomment-5358188654
  background: Form controls like `input`, `textarea`, `button`, and `select` have historically had `box-sizing: border-box` from the very beginning. In addition, authors generally use a reset style of the form `* { box-sizing: border-box }`. If we design our new styles assuming `content-box`, then they’ll break if `border-box` is applied vis the reset stylesheet, and there may be surprising/annoying results for
  irc (36 lines):
    | <ntim> topic https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14249
    | <ntim> fantasai: ...explains the issue (all in the issue)
    | <masonf> +1 to the proposal
    | <ntim> fantasai: proposal is to add box-sizing to all controls
    | <lwarlow> q+
    | <JakeA> q+
    | <JohnJansen> ack lwarlow
    | <JakeA> q-
    | <ntim> lwarlow: I thought we were already doing this on css-forms? normalizing it makes sense
    | <fantasai> We already have it on input, textarea, button, select. This is about adding it to the internals.
    | <emilio> q+
    | <JohnJansen> ack jakea
    | <ntim> JakeA: the textarea issue I have ran into
    | <JohnJansen> ack emilio
    | <ntim> emilio: just to be clear, does this apply to all input types right now? a bit surprising but sounds good
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Add box-sizing to internal form control parts as described in the issue.
    | <ntim> lwarlow: is it only for appearance: base or also for auto?
    | <masonf> q+
    | <ntim> fantasai: i think we can do it unconditionally, already in the ua sheet for html for inputs
    | <ntim> ntim: +1
    | <JohnJansen> ack masonf
    | <ntim> masonf: my +1s are with asterisk of web compat
    | <emilio> Current rule is:
    | <emilio> input:is([type=radio i], [type=checkbox i], [type=reset i], [type=button i],
    | <emilio> [type=submit i], [type=color i], [type=search i]), select, button {
    | <emilio>   box-sizing: border-box;
    | <emilio> }
    | <ntim> fantasai: looks like text related ones don't have it right now
    | <zcorpan> https://searchfox.org/firefox-main/source/layout/style/res/forms.css
    | <lwarlow> +1
    | <ntim> ntim: what do browsers do? they don't follow the spec.
    | <jarhar> +1
    | <masonf> +1
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Add box-sizing to internal form control parts as described in the issue.
    | <lwarlow> Chromium already does this for file-selector-button and that's the only existing one.
    | <fantasai> ACTION: Evaluate <input>
- 2026-08-20 #14250 [css-forms-1]
  RESOLVED: Apply 'font: 100% system-ui' to ::picker-icon and ::checkmark and any other icon pseudos we add in the future
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14250#issuecomment-5358336573
  background: We currently inherit font and all its longhands explicitly, however there are other typesetting properties that we do not inherit because they are currently blocked by the HTML UA default stylesheet, see https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/rendering.html#form-controls which currently says:
  irc (54 lines):
    | <emilio> https://wpt.fyi/results/html/rendering/non-replaced-elements/form-controls/resets.html?label=master&label=experimental&aligned
    | <jarhar> q+
    | <emilio> q+
    | <JohnJansen> ack jarhar
    | <ntim> fantasai: we decided to inherit typography into text controls. The current sheet excludes certain props. For appearance: base, we should definitely inherit stuff, including select. Second thing is to set system fonts/font-style onto ::checkmark / ::picker-icon to avoid putting weird fonts on those.
    | <ntim> jarhar: reshuffling text things to inherit seems fine. Second thing is hopefully fine. We should check if system-ui looks ok on windows / linux
    | <ntim> fantasai: it's definitely going to look better than random fonts
    | <fantasai> input, button, textarea { letter-spacing: initial; word-spacing: initial; line-height: initial;
    | <fantasai> }
    | <ntim> jarhar: what does exactly merging the two style rules mean?
    | <fantasai> input, select, button, textarea { text-transform: initial; text-indent: initial; text-shadow: initial; appearance: auto;
    | <fantasai> }
    | <ntim> fantasai: it would be merging the two above
    | <ntim> masonf: are there changes for appearance: auto or just base?
    | <ntim> fantasai: either way is ok
    | <emilio> Gecko does have select here: https://searchfox.org/firefox-main/rev/ad5db99e4d2e00e9b5db2700c69050d709c7f340/layout/style/res/forms.css#118-129
    | <ntim> masonf: i think our general focus should be appearance: base. auto is probably harder. select is used everywhere, so breakage is very likely
    | <JohnJansen> ack emilio
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: letter-spacing, word-spacing, text-shadow inherit on <select>
    | <lwarlow> q+
    | <masonf> you mean   `s/on <select>/on appearance:base <select>`, right?
    | <ntim> emilio: we probably want to reset most of font on the items
    | <JohnJansen> ack lwarlow
    | <ntim> emilio: gecko already does reset some stuff globally
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: letter-spacing, word-spacing, text-shadow inherit on <select> for appearance: base
    | <ntim> fantasai: auto appearance is resetting stuff to the default value. For appearance: base the intention is to inherit font into the control. Proposal includes multiple things
    | <JohnJansen> q?
    | <jarhar> +1 because we dont have to change appearance:auto
    | <masonf> +1 for the same reason
    | <keithamus> +1
    | <lwarlow> +1
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Apply font-family: system-ui; font-style: normal to ::picker-icon and ::checkmark
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: letter-spacing, word-spacing, text-shadow inherit on <select> for appearance: base
    | <jarhar> +1 but id like to look at windows and linux
    | <keithamus> +1
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Apply font-family: system-ui; font-style: normal to ::picker-icon and ::checkmark and any other icon pseudos we add in the future
    | <emilio> q+
    | <masonf> q+
    | <lwarlow> +1
    | <JohnJansen> ack emilio
    | <fantasai> 'font: 1em system-ui'
    | <ntim> emilio: should we set the font shorthand?
    | <ntim> emilio: there's also other weird font properties
    | <JohnJansen> ack masonf
    | <ntim> masonf: this does change existing current stuff right?
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Apply 'font: 100% system-ui' to ::picker-icon and ::checkmark and any other icon pseudos we add in the future
    | <ntim> fantasai: I suspect people who care about the checkmark probably already changed it
    | <lwarlow> +1
    | <ntim> masonf: you're probably right
    | <ntim> keithamus: do we need this if we're going to use custom icons?
    | <keithamus> +1
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Apply 'font: 100% system-ui' to ::picker-icon and ::checkmark and any other icon pseudos we add in the future

    | <fantasai> for ::checkmark

- 2026-08-26 #14008 [css-align-3,css-anchor-position-1] -> [[anchor-positioning]]
  RESOLVED: Do what fantasai says in the last comment
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14008#issuecomment-5428245947
  background: From: https://drafts.csswg.org/css-align-3/#auto-safety-position
  irc (39 lines):
    | <emilio> fantasai: we discuss at the face to face the sizing / positioning of the select and concluded the behavior we wanted is attach to the safe keyword to the behavior that the abspos tries to stay within the original CB even if that'd overflow its position area CB
    | <emilio> ... this issue is talking about behavior when safe / unsafe are not specified
    | <emilio> ... there's a proposal that if it's possible to scroll in a particular direction we try to maintain alignment
    | <emilio> ... e.g. you have an anchor in the middle of the viewport and a box with position-area to be below it but too big. If you can scroll to reach it we keep it below the anchor
    | <emilio> ... that's the proposed behavior for the default (not safe or unsafe)
    | <emilio> ... unsafe will keep it under the anchor
    | <emilio> ... safe will make it sure it fits within the viewport
    | <emilio> iank_: so difference between blink / webkit is removing a restriction that this only happens for things with a default anchor, is that correct?
    | <emilio> fantasai: not sure what chrome is doing
    | <emilio> ... what I wanted to implement in webkit is to keep the element in the requested position if you can scroll to it
    | <emilio> ... if there's scrollable area it'll allow you to scroll
    | <emilio> iank_: so the proposal is for default safety to allow to overflow up until the scrollable limit
    | <emilio> ... for abspos the scrollable limit is infinity in the scrollable direction
    | <emilio> ... for fixed pos is the end scrollable padding edge
    | <emilio> fantasai: not sure it's infinity, if you have a abspos anchor attached to the element
    | <emilio> iank_: just meant for an abspos
    | <emilio> fantasai: I think so
    | <emilio> iank_: for the scrollable direction, gets clamped if it goes into the scrollable direction
    | <emilio> ... for this we need to stop paying attention to the default anchor
    | <dshin-moz> r+
    | <emilio> fantasai: you need to because if scrolling can't change the position of the anchor
    | <dshin-moz> q+
    | <emilio> ... then you can't see it
    | <emilio> ... "if you can scroll to see it then you don't pull it out of alignment"
    | <emilio> iank_: we can't determine that prior to... the decision point has to be default anchor
    | <emilio> fantasai: for fixedpos you also know the extent of the scrollable content area
    | <emilio> iank_: so this is basically what webkit and blink are already doing
    | <astearns> ack dshin-moz
    | <emilio> dshin-moz: maybe I'm missing something but where do we specify safe / unsafe?
    | <emilio> fantasai: on the alignment property
    | <emilio> dshin-moz: so you need align/justify-self: normal unsafe or so?
    | <emilio> fantasai: yes
    | <emilio> dshin-moz: but doesn't override the alignment keyword by position-area?
    | <emilio> fantasai: can combine safe / unsafe with normal
    | <emilio> dshin-moz: so unsafe normal then position-area center ??
    | <emilio> dshin-moz: so alignment is `unsafe normal` then `position-area: center center`, then `position-area` will override `normal` to `anchor-center`, but then look at the safety keyword
    | <emilio> fantasai: yes
    | <emilio> PROPOSED: Do what fantasai says in the last comment
    | <emilio> RESOLVED: Do what fantasai says in the last comment
- 2026-08-26 #14255 [css-forms-1]
  RESOLVED: Move 'white-space: nowrap' from 'select option' to 'select' (not !important)
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14255#issuecomment-5427718045
  background: Currently if the contents of an option are too long but wrappable, they will wrap onto multiple lines. But this can disturb the general page layout, so option text is typically restricted to one line in a `<select>` control. We should disable wrapping for the base appearance in-page select button text also, to help maintain this height expectation, by moving `white-space: nowrap` from `option` to 
  irc (60 lines):
    | <kbabbitt> fantasai: some very nice illustrations from joey that show ... when the text of the select is too long
    | <kbabbitt> .... it overflows and that looks stupid
    | <kbabbitt> ... we would like to not do that
    | <kbabbitt> ... what we're proposing to do is clip it, have both select and selected content apply clipping
    | <kbabbitt> ... slightly more complicated part is, in current UA stylesheet for select, we set white-space no-wrap on options
    | <kbabbitt> ... when we put option text into select button it is not styled with option style rules
    | <kbabbitt> .... we want to delete the option so it continues to not wraP
    | <kbabbitt> ... we don't want things wrapping in select unless author requested it
    | <kbabbitt> ... also selected content is kind of flex-y so we want to allow it to shink down to 0 to make room for picker icon
    | <kbabbitt> ... there are additional complications when selected content does not exist, that's next issue we already resolved by creating pseudo element
    | <kbabbitt> ... so it's the pseudo we want to style here
    | <astearns> q?
    | <kbabbitt> ... several pieces to the proposal here for controlling overflow nicely
    | <kbabbitt> ... one is white-space no-wrap to selected option
    | <emilio> q+
    | <kbabbitt> ... set overflow clip on select button itself and field content
    | <fantasai> see https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14323
    | <kbabbitt> ... pseudo element
    | <kbabbitt> ... third part is to set min inline size on it to 0 so it can shrink down when there's not space and leave room for picker icon
    | <kbabbitt> ... there's a 4th part we can talk about later
    | <kbabbitt> emilio: fwiw Gecko already applies white-space no-wrap on select with important
    | <kbabbitt> ... fine if we want to remove important for appearance base, maybe
    | <lwarlow> Generally I think the less !important the better
    | <kbabbitt> astearns: shall we decide on white-space part first?
    | <kbabbitt> fantasai: yes let's take 1 at a time
    | <CameronPsychpsyo> q+
    | <masonf> 1+
    | <CameronPsychpsyo> q-
    | <kbabbitt> astearns: any opinion on whether it should be !important for appearance base?
    | <miriam> +1
    | <kbabbitt> ... think 'no' so authors can override it
    | <lwarlow> Yeah I "veto" important.
    | <davidjmarland> +1
    | <masonf> Not sure what 1+ was but I meant to say "no"
    | <miriam> that it should not be important
    | <emilio> Sg
    | <kbabbitt> ... anyone with concerns about applying white-space: no-wrap to select in appearance: base at all?
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Move 'white-space: nowrap' from 'select option' to 'select' (not !important)
    | <masonf> +1
    | <lwarlow> +1
    | <kbabbitt> ... objections to applying that non-important?
    | <kbabbitt> jarhar: are we also going to do this for appearance auto if web compatible?
    | <lwarlow> Chrome uses pre for appearance: auto currently
    | <kbabbitt> emilio: what do you do for... kind of need to on appearance auto right?
    | <kbabbitt> fantasai: gecko is doing it already, that shows it's web compatible
    | <kbabbitt> astearns: and hopefully the important part is not needed for web compat
    | <kbabbitt> emilio: hopefully not
    | <kbabbitt> lwarlow: is there a big differenc between pre and no-wrap?
    | <kbabbitt> fantasai: difference is whether white space is preferred
    | <kbabbitt> ... now that all of this is CSS formatted I don't think we want to preserve whitespasce for indentation etc
    | <kbabbitt> emilio: right, if option has newline don't want it
    | <kbabbitt> astearns: calling for objections again to proposed resolution
    | <CameronPsychpsyo> The fact that it's inherited is a little worrying for web compat
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Move 'white-space: nowrap' from 'select option' to 'select' (not !important)
    | <fantasai> CameronPsychpsyo, it's already applied to option.
    | <kbabbitt> astearns: next part
    | <kbabbitt> fantasai: next part is applying overflow: clip to select button and to selected option
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: select, selectedcontent, select::field-content { overflow: clip; }
    | <kbabbitt> ... reason we apply it to both outer and inner thing is because it looks stupid if either of them overflow
    | <kbabbitt> ... don't want to overflow button, also don't want to push picker button outside of icon

- 2026-08-26 #14316 [css-forms-1]
  RESOLVED: ::Picker-icon and ::checkmark are not in the a11y tree
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14316#issuecomment-5427379486
  background: The icons we use for ::checkmark and ::picker-icon are not particularly useful to read aloud. The status of the control is already exposed to AT independently, so we propose using empty alt text for these glyphs:
  irc (60 lines):
    | <lwarlow> q+
    | <kbabbitt> fantasai: this issue is about alt text for a picker icon and checkmark
    | <kbabbitt> ... basic idea is that state of select is represented in tree attr already
    | <kbabbitt> ... leaving out representation of icon is not necessary or desired
    | <kbabbitt> ... in our stylesheet proposal we put an empty alt string on these pseudos
    | <kbabbitt> ... looks like in Chromium they just remove them from the a11y tree unconditionally
    | <kbabbitt> ... either of these options would work
    | <kbabbitt> ... not sure which approach we want to take
    | <masonf> q+
    | <astearns> ack lwarlow
    | <kbabbitt> ... seems like we ahve agreement that ti shouldn't be in a11y tree
    | <kbabbitt> lwarlow: slight preference for Chromium approach, that these shouldn't be exposed
    | <masonf> q-
    | <kbabbitt> ... if someone changes the content, making them remember they have to add empty string so they don't showup might be a footgun
    | <kbabbitt> ... can't think of a situation where you'd want them exposed
    | <kbabbitt> ... e.g. for checkbox checked state is already exposed
    | <masonf> +1 to what Luke said
    | <kbabbitt> ... feel like it would probably end up getting noisy
    | <kbabbitt> also +1 to lwarlow
    | <kbabbitt> fantasai: makes sense
    | <dbaron> +1 to Luke -- since I think it's going to be relatively common to change 'content' for these pseudos
    | <kbabbitt> .... proposed resolution, these pseudos don't appear in a11y tree unconditionally
    | <kbabbitt> ... we can double check with a11y group to make sure they're happy
    | <kbabbitt> astearns: just checkmark and picker-icon or other fom control pseudos?
    | <kbabbitt> fantasai: think it's just these, others represent some functionality
    | <lwarlow> q+
    | <kbabbitt> ... we can certainly revisit when we get to the point where we have deeper integration
    | <kbabbitt> ... maybe the slider ones
    | <kbabbitt> lwarlow: I think generally for pseudos you don't want them exposed
    | <kbabbitt> ... increment button and decrement button
    | <kbabbitt> ... not keybaord accessible nor for assistive techs, those use commands
    | <kbabbitt> ... reveal icon is probably a similar situation, should be an action
    | <kbabbitt> fantasai: user can disable these?
    | <fantasai> s/user/author
    | <kbabbitt> lwarlow: I think they're unconditionally in the a11y tree right now
    | <kbabbitt> ... maybe not with display:none
    | <masonf> ::file-selector-button?
    | <kbabbitt> ... maybe be display:none until you hover and then it's visible, don't want hover state to affect it
    | <kbabbitt> ... [missed]
    | <kbabbitt> ... file input has an activation behavior, file selector is a ? button
    | <kbabbitt> ... that's definitely one to double check
    | <kbabbitt> ... I suspect most we don't want to expose
    | <kbabbitt> astearns: if we take a resolution to go with Chrome's behavior for these 2 and make a list of others, and say what do you think about that...?
    | <kbabbitt> lwarlow: that works, can take an action item to look
    | <JohnJansen> +1 to taking the list to ARIA
    | <kbabbitt> ... might be that browsers already agree
    | <emilio> q+
    | <kbabbitt> astearns: would like to get their opinion on this
    | <kbabbitt> ... only concern is, ifwe come up with a use case for adding more info to a checkmark somehow, is this a reversible thing?
    | <kbabbitt> ... can we expose the checkmark?
    | <kbabbitt> fantasai: at that point authors would have to go back an dfix alt text
    | <astearns> ack lwarlow
    | <kbabbitt> ... css speech has speech property which lets you enable and disable content for speech purposes
    | <kbabbitt> ... would give an independently cascading switch so people don't have to fix their content
    | <fantasai> https://www.w3.org/TR/css-speech-1/#speaking-props-speak
    | <lwarlow> I think we could make it so that content: "foo" isn't included but if they do content: "foo" /"explicit" then it works
    | <kbabbitt> astearns: this is just future problems
    | <fantasai> lwarlow, oh interesting ...
    | <kbabbitt> emilio: +1 to this but can we define the mechanism that does this, e.g. these elements have aria-hidden
    | <lwarlow> q+
- 2026-08-26 #14319 [css-forms-1]
  RESOLVED: input, select, option { align-content: unsafe center; align-items: unsafe center; }
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14319#issuecomment-5427495924
  background: The UA stylesheet currently applies block-axis centering in a few places: options, radio buttons, checkboxes, and [buttons](https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14190). But actually, when an input or select is sized larger than the text and icons it contains, its contents should also be centered.
  related: #14190 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14190
  irc (60 lines):
    | <emilio> scribe+
    | <emilio> fantasai: so UA sheet applies block-axis centering in a few places but when an input / select is sized larger content ends up start-aligned
    | <emilio> ... typically you want it to be centered
    | <emilio> ... so we want to suggest applying justify-/align-content center to option, select, and input
    | <flackr> q+
    | <CameronPsychpsyo> Not justify, I think
    | <emilio> ... buttons currently have center, might want to change it
    | <emilio> ... might not need to change that one
    | <dbaron> Issue says align-content/items but not justify-*
    | <emilio> s/justify-/align-items/
    | <emilio> q+
    | <emilio> fantasai: button looks like it has some complications
    | <astearns> ack flackr
    | <dbaron> Do the button complications apply to <input type=button> ?
    | <emilio> flackr: can you clarify safe vs. unsafe?
    | <emilio> fantasai: safe centering start aligns when overflowing
    | <emilio> flackr: but what are you proposing?
    | <emilio> fantasai: for input / select it should be unsafe center
    | <emilio> ... let's sure for option
    | <emilio> flackr: is it better to see the start?
    | <emilio> fantasai: not if it's a single line, if you're going to clip something you want to clip the edges
    | <emilio> ... that's why for options I'm not really sure
    | <emilio> ... for input / select we do want unsafe center
    | <lwarlow> q+
    | <emilio> flackr: it seems about flaw of self-alignment that we don't have the single / multi line distinction? But sounds good
    | <emilio> fantasai: most things on CSS are not confined to a single line
    | <emilio> flackr: I guess in general the argument for safe centering is that you want to see the first line, anyway
    | <emilio> ... seems fine
    | <fantasai> scribe+
    | <fantasai> scribe+ kbabbit
    | <kbabbitt> emilio: seems fine I think we decided, for input at least, and maybe select, align-content is already a thing in HTML spec for buttons, we may need something else for compat
    | <kbabbitt> fantasai: yeah you mentioned that in other issue
    | <kbabbitt> emilio: in general seems fine, would try to do it regardless of appearance, align-content is already there
    | <kbabbitt> fantasai: for input and select I think we can do it unconditionally
    | <kbabbitt> astearns: so I'm clear, input and select already have align-content: center
    | <kbabbitt> emilio: very recently, only implemented in Firefox
    | <kbabbitt> ... but in general the only breakage we've noticed is buttons
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: input, select { align-content: unsafe center; align-items: unsafe center; } option { align-content: center; align-items: center; }
    | <kbabbitt> ... people love to all:unset on their buttons
    | <kbabbitt> astearns: did you do this for option as well, or is it in spec?
    | <fantasai> [excluding the button types]
    | <kbabbitt> emilio: no, for options it only makes sense in customizable select
    | <kbabbitt> ... option could be a bit risker for listboxes, those actually have real CSS boxes
    | <kbabbitt> ... for non-custom select I don't think it matters
    | <kbabbitt> astearns: in your comment you say you're not convinced by align-items, do you have a concern there?
    | <fantasai> or maybe 'safe center' for option
    | <kbabbitt> ... for input selects and options are you ok with align center?
    | <astearns> q?
    | <kbabbitt> emilio: yes I think that'd be fine, thought it wasn't needed, but it is for flex
    | <astearns> ack emilio
    | <astearns> ack lwarlow
    | <emilio> lwarlow: Is this all inputs?
    | <emilio> ... should this be just textual inputs?
    | <emilio> ... presumably not range / button inputs?
    | <emilio> ... guessing fine for all but
    | <emilio> fantasai: unsure about the button ones, but they are just text
    | <emilio> ... for checkmark / etc we actually want them centered there
    | <emilio> ... there's a bit of a compat consideration for checkbox if they use appearance: none
    | <emilio> ... but we can try and revert if needed
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: input, select { align-content: unsafe center; align-items: unsafe center; } option { align-content: safe center; align-items: safe center; }
- 2026-08-26 #14338 [css-mixins-1]
  RESOLVED: Function arguments (including defaults), as well as locals and result, are evaluated in the context in which the function is called.
  https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14338#issuecomment-5427887665
  background: Take this example:
  irc (60 lines):
    | <emilio> TabAtkins: see the issue example
    | <emilio> ... it shows the different behavior for passing it arguments
    | <emilio> ... in particular the random() function but anything that has computation
    | <emilio> ... for custom functions we create a property
    | <emilio> ... so whatever you pass is computed based on that hypothetical element
    | <emilio> ... that's not the case for regular functions
    | <emilio> ... e.g. if you write 1em it gets resolved against that element
    | <emilio> ... seems unfortunate
    | <emilio> ... turns out chrome did not implement what the spec says
    | <emilio> ... it seems they're evaluating the arguments in the context of where the function is called
    | <emilio> ... in the example with --hypot() behaves as if they had two number arguments
    | <emilio> ... the example uses random() but also ems and such are affected
    | <emilio> ... needs to know whether you're in the font-size property or what not
    | <emilio> ... e.g. if you use 1em in a custom function in font-size they resolve against the parent
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Function arguments are evaluated in the context in which the function is called.
    | <lea> +1, the opposite seems extremely bizarre
    | <emilio> ... proposal is to evaluate the arguments in the context outside the function
    | <emilio> ... generally would make stuff more like people would expect
    | <ydaniv> +1
    | <emilio> ... currently planned to keep function default arguments
    | <lea> q+
    | <lea> q-
    | <emilio> ... but I could be convinced
    | <lea> q+
    | <emilio> ... thoughts?
    | <emilio> fantasai: Antti replied that default argument would be confusing and hard to implement if it wasn't evaluated in the outside context
    | <astearns> ack fantasai
    | <emilio> ... also note that chrome resolves locals and the result descriptor in the outside context
    | <emilio> TabAtkins: that doesn't make any sense to me
    | <emilio> ... what's the inside context even for in that case?
    | <emilio> fantasai: for your convenience
    | <emilio> TabAtkins: what does it even do in that case?
    | <emilio> q+
    | <fantasai> emilio: It's just a scoping mechanism
    | <kbabbitt> scribe+
    | <kbabbitt> emilio: keeps locals, it's a scope effectively
    | <emilio> ack emilio
    | <kbabbitt> ... it is weird
    | <emilio> astearns: should we consider the locals stuff elsewhere?
    | <emilio> TabAtkins: ok chrome's behavior is weird
    | <emilio> ... I'm willing to take this as a tentative evaluation
    | <astearns> ack lea
    | <emilio> s/evaluation/resolution
    | <fantasai> PROPOSED: Function arguments (including defaults), as well as locals and result, are evaluated in the context in which the function is called.
    | <emilio> lea: first, huge +1
    | <emilio> ... I'm confused about not changing the defaults
    | <emilio> ... that seems surprising, what's the rationale?
    | <emilio> TabAtkins: happy to make them consistent
    | <emilio> ... seems to be what impls are going for anyway
    | <emilio> ... I thought the inner context would be easier to reason about
    | <fantasai> You don't have a local font-size, Tab. How are you going to compute 1em? ...
    | <emilio> ... seems impls went for that but didn't tell me
    | <emilio> astearns: are there multiple impls?
    | <TabAtkins> yes you do, the ehypothetical element inherits from the element being invoked on
    | <emilio> TabAtkins: chrome ships, safari is implementing
    | <emilio> PROPOSED: [what fantasai wrote up thread]
    | <fantasai> RESOLVED: Function arguments (including defaults), as well as locals and result, are evaluated in the context in which the function is called.
    | <CameronPsychpsyo> Ladybird is working on custom functions as well IIRC
    | <lea> also this means that they don't need to be computationally independent, like regular initial values
    | <romain> Safari is shipping

- #14372 [css-mixins] Can we drop the "anonymous function" idea for mixin evaluation? (opened 2026-08-21) https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14372
    background: A while back, in #12927, we defined the evaluation model of mixins to, under the covers, wrap all the property values set by the mixin into anonymous functions. At the time, this let us move away from `env()`-based parameters/locals and instead use the existing custom-function `var()`-based parameters/locals. Functions had a nice "hypothetical element" model that let us use the author-chosen param
    related: #12927 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/12927, #14004 https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14004

    background: Transformation of non-SVG boxes seem to be ignored by view timeline computation, which makes sense to me given we [use](https://drafts.csswg.org/scroll-animations-1/#view-timelines) the principal box of the subject.

- #14328 [css-inline-3] Does `text-box-trim` remove negative leading? (opened 2026-08-14) https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/14328
    background: When the `line-height` is smaller than the font's ascent/descent, line boxes can have negative leading. If `text-box-trim` is applied to a block using a taller edge metric, does it remove this negative leading, thereby increasing the height of the text box? Blink and Gecko do this, but WebKit doesn't — see this [example](https://codepen.io/editor/kbhomes/pen/019ffe30-e39c-73f2-8973-252882a621c3).
